#### F-012 · Stored XSS

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/bac/index.php:71`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** An authenticated user requests the BAC page with action and `user_id` set and a header X-Forwarded-For: &lt;svg `onload=alert`(1)&gt;. The value is stored in `bac_log.ip_address`, and `bac/index.php` renders it unescaped for every user who views the access log.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```
  69 │         $html .= "<td>{$log['accessor_user']} (ID: {$log['user_id']})</td>";
  70 │         $html .= "<td>{$target_user}</td>";
→ 71 │         $html .= "<td>{$log['ip_address']}</td>";
  72 │         $html .= "<td>{$log['timestamp']}</td>";
  73 │         $html .= "</tr>";
```

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◕ [M-020](#m-020) — HTML-escape log fields when rendering access log

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-013"></a><a id="f-013"></a>
#### F-013 · DOM-based XSS from URL default parameter written

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/xss_d/index.php:53`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** At `vulnerabilities/xss_d/index.php:53`, an inline script takes the text after 'default=' from `location.href`, decodes it with `decodeURI`, and writes it unencoded with `document.write`. An attacker sends a logged-in victim a link to the `xss_d` page, such as ?default=`<script>`...&lt;`/script`&gt; or ?default=&lt;`/option`&gt;&lt;`/select`&gt;&lt;img `src=x` `onerror=alert`(`document.cookie`)&gt;.

The impossible level drops `decodeURI`.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - Line 52 takes lang from `document.location.href`, and line 53 writes it decoded into HTML with `document.write`. `medium.php` blocks only '&lt;script'. `high.php` allowlists only the server-visible query, so a URL fragment bypasses it.

```
  51 │ 					if (document.location.href.indexOf("default=") >= 0) {
  52 │ 						var lang = document.location.href.substring(document.location.href.indexOf("default=")+8);
→ 53 │ 						document.write("<option value='" + lang + "'>" + $decodeURI(lang) + "</option>");
  54 │ 						document.write("<option value='' disabled='disabled'>----</option>");
  55 │ 					}
```

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◕ [M-021](#m-021) — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer (🟠 [F-013](#f-013))

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04)

<a id="t-025"></a><a id="f-025"></a>
#### F-025 · CSRF check bypass

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/csrf/source/medium.php:5`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Cookie-authenticated changes lack request authenticity checks

**Issue:** The medium level accepts a request if `SERVER_NAME` appears anywhere in the Referer. A Referer from an attacker-controlled origin whose hostname contains that string passes the check.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - A state-changing request can be triggered cross-origin without a synchroniser token.

**Fix:** Enforce a same-origin or signed CSRF token on every state-changing endpoint → ◕ [M-032](#m-032) — Replace Referer substring check with anti-CSRF token

**Classification:** Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-026"></a><a id="f-026"></a>
#### F-026 · All repository secrets dumped base64-encoded to workflow

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `.github/workflows/vulnerable.yml:54`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-014](#w-014) - All repository secrets dumped base64-encoded to workflow logs

**Issue:** On every push to master the `get_secrets` job serialises all repository secrets via `toJSON(secrets)` into ALLMYSECRETS and echoes it through base64. GitHub log masking only matches the literal secret values, so the encoded blob is shown in clear.

Anyone able to read the Actions logs (any user if the repository is public) decodes it and obtains every secret, including `DVWA_SECRET_KEY` and the run's `GITHUB_TOKEN`.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - Line 31 sets ALLMYSECRETS to `toJSON(secrets)`; lines 51 and 54 pipe `SUPER_SECRET` and ALLMYSECRETS through base64 into the log, defeating masking. Triggered on push to master (lines 3-6). Log readership depends on repository visibility, not shown in source.

```yaml
  52 │
  53 │           # Same for all the tokens.
→ 54 │           echo "$ALLMYSECRETS" | base64
```

**Fix:** Strip secrets and PII from every log sink and rotate any token that already leaked → ◕ [M-033](#m-033) — Remove secret dumping step from vulnerable workflow

**Classification:** Missing Audit Logging & Accountability · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-532](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/532.html) · [OWASP A09:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A09_2025-Security_Logging_and_Alerting_Failures/)

### 🟡 Medium (45)

<a id="t-040"></a><a id="f-040"></a>
#### F-040 · Hardcoded weak MariaDB root and application credentials

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - MariaDB Database  ·  **Location:** `compose.yml:31`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-007](#w-007) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Issue:** An attacker who gets code execution or network access inside the dvwa Docker network can log in to MariaDB as root with the published password 'dvwa', or as the dvwa user with the committed password. They then get full read/write over every database, including the users table and its password hashes.

Anyone with these credentials can log in to the DB as root, with full control of DVWA data and the server configuration.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `compose.yml:31` sets `MYSQL_ROOT_PASSWORD`=dvwa and line 34 sets a literal 8-char `MYSQL_PASSWORD`, both committed in plaintext.

**Fix:** Move the credential out of source control into a secret store and rotate it → ◑ [M-044](#m-044) — Use Docker secrets with strong random DB passwords

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-043"></a><a id="f-043"></a>
#### F-043 · Hardcoded encryption key and static secret allow API token

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/api/src/Token.php:10`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-007](#w-007) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Issue:** An attacker who knows the published source encrypts `{"secret":"12345","expires":9999999999}` with AES-128-GCM using the embedded key 'Paintbrush'. OrderController then accepts the forged bearer token as a valid access token.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Move the cryptographic key out of source control into a managed secret store and rotate it → ◑ [M-047](#m-047) — Load token signing key from secret store

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-321](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-051"></a><a id="f-051"></a>
#### F-051 · Hardcoded client secret and user password in API login

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/api/src/LoginController.php:96`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-007](#w-007) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Issue:** An attacker who reads the source uses client 1471.`dvwa.digi.ninja` with secret ABigLongSecret and user mrbennett with password becareful. The login endpoint then issues valid access and refresh tokens for the order API.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Move the credential out of source control into a secret store and rotate it → ◑ [M-055](#m-055) — Verify API credentials against hashed user store

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-052"></a><a id="f-052"></a>
#### F-052 · Static access token accepted from the URL query string

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/bac/source/medium.php:26`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHN-07](#tc-authn-07))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-007](#w-007) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Issue:** An unauthorized caller adds `token=user_token` to the profile request URL. The server compares it with a hardcoded constant and returns any user's profile. The denial message itself reveals the value, and the token also lands in URLs and logs.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - if (`isset($_GET['token'])` && `$_GET['token']` == 'user_token') grants access. Line 46 tells the caller to use `token=user_token`.

**Fix:** Move the credential out of source control into a secret store and rotate it → ◑ [M-056](#m-056) — Replace the constant URL token with session authorization

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-07](#tc-authn-07)

<a id="t-003"></a><a id="f-003"></a>
#### F-003 · Raw Database Error Disclosure to Client

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (5)

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Instances (5):** `login.php`: 40; `vulnerabilities/authbypass/change_user_details.php`: 48; `vulnerabilities/brute/source/low.php`: 13; `vulnerabilities/sqli/source/low.php`: 11; `vulnerabilities/xss_s/source/low.php`: 17

**Issue:** An attacker submits malformed input. When the query fails, `die()` prints `mysqli_error`, which exposes SQL syntax and schema details that help with injection.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Replace developer error pages with a generic message in production responses → ◑ [M-012](#m-012) — Return generic error messages to clients

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-209](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/209.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/)

<a id="t-041"></a><a id="f-041"></a>
#### F-041 · Login lacks attempt throttling or lockout

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `login.php:41`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** An attacker repeatedly fetches the login token and POSTs password guesses to `login.php`; no counter, delay or lockout limits attempts, enabling online brute force against weak seeded passwords.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Apply rate limiting and lock-out thresholds on authentication endpoints → ◑ [M-045](#m-045) — Add login rate limiting and lockout

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-307](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/307.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-042"></a><a id="f-042"></a>
#### F-042 · No login attempt limiting on brute-force form

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/brute/source/low.php:12`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** An attacker scripts unlimited username and password guesses against the GET login and recovers account passwords. The low level has no throttling, and the medium and high levels only add short sleeps.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Apply rate limiting and lock-out thresholds on authentication endpoints → ◑ [M-046](#m-046) — Add per-account and per-IP login throttling

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-307](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/307.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-044"></a><a id="f-044"></a>
#### F-044 · Session fixation

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `dvwa/includes/dvwaPage.inc.php:103`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** Below impossible level, `dvwa_start_session` reuses the session id from the request cookie at login, so an attacker who plants a known session id in a victim browser hijacks the authenticated session.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-048](#m-048) — Rotate the session identifier on authentication

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-384](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/384.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-045"></a><a id="f-045"></a>
#### F-045 · Debug CAPTCHA backdoor left in production code

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/captcha/source/high.php:20`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** An attacker reads the DEV NOTE comment that `captcha/index.php` renders. The attacker then sends `g-recaptcha-response=hidd3n_valu3` with User-Agent: `reCAPTCHA`, which passes the CAPTCHA check and changes the password automatically.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-049](#m-049) — Remove hardcoded CAPTCHA bypass branch

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-489](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/489.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-046"></a><a id="f-046"></a>
#### F-046 · Login passwords submitted in GET query strings

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/brute/source/low.php:8`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHN-07](#tc-authn-07))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-015](#w-015) - Login passwords submitted in GET query strings (`low.php:8`)

**Issue:** The brute-force login forms send username and password as GET parameters. Passwords are recorded in Apache access logs, browser history, proxies and `Referer` headers. Anyone with access to those records can reuse them to log in.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - \$pass = `$_GET['password']` in brute `low.php:8`, `medium.php:9` and `high.php:13`. The handlers trigger on `$_GET['Login']`.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-050](#m-050) — Accept login credentials only in POST bodies over TLS

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-598](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/598.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-07](#tc-authn-07)

<a id="t-050"></a><a id="f-050"></a>
#### F-050 · CAPTCHA state trusted from client-supplied field

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/captcha/source/medium.php:55`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** A bot posts `step=2` with `passed_captcha=true`. The server trusts this client-supplied flag and changes the password without a solved CAPTCHA.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-054](#m-054) — Enforce authorization on the server

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-602](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/602.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-053"></a><a id="f-053"></a>
#### F-053 · CAPTCHA bypass by submitting step 2 directly

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/captcha/source/low.php:45`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** A bot posts `step=2` with `password_new` and `password_conf` directly. Step 2 never checks that the CAPTCHA was solved, so the bot changes the password with no human verification.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-057](#m-057) — Verify CAPTCHA in the same request as the change

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-841](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/841.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-054"></a><a id="f-054"></a>
#### F-054 · API-created users all receive the same default password

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/api/src/UserController.php:167`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHN-04](#tc-authn-04))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** A caller creates a user through POST `/vulnerabilities/api/v2/user/`. The new user's password is always `sha256('password')`, a fixed known value hashed with a fast, unsalted hash. Anyone who knows the default can authenticate as any newly created user wherever this hash is checked.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `addUser` builds new `User(..., hash("sha256", "password"))`. The seeded users at lines 31-33 also hold bare `SHA-256` hashes.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-058](#m-058) — Require a caller-chosen password hashed with password_hash

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-1393](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1393.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-04](#tc-authn-04)

<a id="t-067"></a><a id="f-067"></a>
#### F-067 · Access log trusts client X-Forwarded-For as source IP

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Instances (2):** `vulnerabilities/bac/source/high.php`: 92; `vulnerabilities/bac/source/impossible.php`: 188

**Issue:** A user probing other profiles sets X-Forwarded-For to an arbitrary address. The BAC log records that forged value instead of `REMOTE_ADDR`, so the user's unauthorized access attempts are attributed to another host.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-070](#m-070) — Log REMOTE_ADDR unless proxy is trusted

**Classification:** Missing Audit Logging & Accountability · STRIDE: Repudiation · [CWE-348](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/348.html) · [OWASP A09:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A09_2025-Security_Logging_and_Alerting_Failures/)

<a id="t-068"></a><a id="f-068"></a>
#### F-068 · Legacy v1 user API discloses password hashes

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/api/src/User.php:45`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker requests GET `/vulnerabilities/api/v1/user/`. The version taken from the URL selects `toArray` case 1, which includes each user's unsalted `SHA-256` password hash for offline cracking.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Restrict the response to the minimum fields needed and never echo secrets → ◑ [M-071](#m-071) — Stop exposing internal information to clients

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-200](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/)

<a id="t-069"></a><a id="f-069"></a>
#### F-069 · Repeating-key XOR used as message encryption

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/cryptography/source/low.php:35`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-008](#w-008) - Security-sensitive data uses weak cryptographic primitives

**Issue:** An attacker encodes known plaintext, XORs it with the output to recover the key 'wachtwoord', and decrypts the intercepted message to obtain the password.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Replace the broken algorithm with a vetted modern primitive (AES-GCM / Argon2id / Ed25519) → ◑ [M-072](#m-072) — Replace the weak cryptographic algorithm

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-327](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/327.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-070"></a><a id="f-070"></a>
#### F-070 · Broken hash primitive

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/captcha/source/impossible.php:19`

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: md5 on line 19 hashes `password_conf` only for an equality check against the new password. No storage or integrity use is shown there.

**Weakness:** [W-008](#w-008) - Security-sensitive data uses weak cryptographic primitives

**Issue:** `MD5`/SHA-1 are collision-broken; unsafe for passwords or integrity of untrusted data.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

**Fix:** Replace the broken hash with a salted password-hashing function (bcrypt/Argon2id) → ◑ [M-004](#m-004) — Manual review: verify Broken hash primitive PHP · ◕ [M-011](#m-011) — Use modern cryptographic hash and KDF algorithms

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-328](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/328.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-04](#tc-authn-04), [TC-CRYPTO-01](#tc-crypto-01) · Rules: CRYPTO-PHP-001

<a id="t-071"></a><a id="f-071"></a>
#### F-071 · Config backup with DB credentials copied into web root

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `dvwa/includes/DBMS/MySQL.php:138`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** Setup copies `config/config.inc.php` to `config.inc.php.bak` under the web root; Apache serves .bak as plain text, so an attacker requests it and reads database credentials.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-073](#m-073) — Remove config backup from web root

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-530](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/530.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/)

<a id="t-072"></a><a id="f-072"></a>
#### F-072 · Attacker-triggerable account lockout

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/brute/source/impossible.php:88`

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: Failed logins increment `failed_login` per username (line 88), but the lockout threshold and \$account_locked logic lie outside the supplied window.

**Issue:** An attacker submits three wrong passwords for a victim's username every 15 minutes. `failed_login` stays at or above the threshold, so the victim cannot log in even with the correct password. Each failure also holds a worker for 2-4 seconds.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

**Fix:** ◑ [M-005](#m-005) — Manual review: verify Attacker-triggerable account lockout · ◑ [M-074](#m-074) — Throttle by client and avoid hard per-user lockout

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-645](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/645.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-076"></a><a id="f-076"></a>
#### F-076 · Help page evals contents

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium - elevated to High as a verified attack-chain keystone in AC-T-006 (Remote Code Execution via Server-Side Injection); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases)  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/view_help.php:20`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-INPUT-03](#tc-input-03), [TC-FILE-01](#tc-file-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Weakness:** [W-010](#w-010) - Application data crosses into executable code

**Issue:** An authenticated user calls `view_help.php` with id and locale values that contain ../ sequences. The server reads any file whose path ends in `help/help.php` or help/help.`<locale>`.php and runs it through eval, so a file the attacker controls at such a path is executed as PHP.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - \$_GET id and locale are interpolated into the `file_get_contents` path, and the result is passed to eval with no allow-list or containment check. Exploitation needs an attacker-writable file at a matching help/ path, which the read sources do not show.

**Fix:** Replace runtime code generation (eval/Function/template render) with a data-only execution path → ◕ [M-076](#m-076) — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input

**Classification:** Code Execution via Unsafe Deserialization or Eval · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-94](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/94.html) · [OWASP A08:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A08_2025-Software_or_Data_Integrity_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-03](#tc-input-03), [TC-FILE-01](#tc-file-01)

<a id="t-077"></a><a id="f-077"></a>
#### F-077 · Eval of help file chosen by request path

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/view_help.php:20`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** An authenticated user controls the id and locale values that build the path of a file whose contents are passed to eval. Any reachable file named `help/help.php` under a traversed directory is executed as PHP.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-077](#m-077) — Allowlist help module ids and include safely

**Classification:** Code Execution via Unsafe Deserialization or Eval · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-98](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/98.html) · [OWASP A08:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A08_2025-Software_or_Data_Integrity_Failures/)

<a id="t-078"></a><a id="f-078"></a>
#### F-078 · Image upload keeps original name without re-encoding

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/upload/source/high.php:20`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-016](#w-016) - Image upload keeps original name without re-encoding (`high.php:20`)

**Issue:** An authenticated user uploads an image-valid polyglot file with embedded script content. It is stored unmodified under its original name, where another inclusion flaw could execute it.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Validate uploaded file type, size, and storage path; never execute uploaded content → ◑ [M-078](#m-078) — Re-encode images and store under random names

**Classification:** Insecure File Handling · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-434](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/434.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-079"></a><a id="f-079"></a>
#### F-079 · Security controls gated by client-controlled cookie

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `dvwa/includes/dvwaPage.inc.php:205`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** `dvwaSecurityLevelGet` returns the security cookie, which selects whether hardened or vulnerable code and cookie flags apply; any user sets `security=low` to disable protections.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-079](#m-079) — Store security level server-side

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-565](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/565.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-080"></a><a id="f-080"></a>
#### F-080 · Profile authorization trusts client user_id

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/bac/source/low.php:33`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** An attacker sets the `user_id` cookie to a victim's ID and requests that ID. The check compares two values the attacker controls, so the attacker can view any user's profile.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-080](#m-080) — Authorize against server-side session user

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-565](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/565.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-081"></a><a id="f-081"></a>
#### F-081 · IDOR guarded by static public token

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium - verified attack-chain keystone in AC-T-002 (Bulk Data Exfiltration via Broken Object Authorization); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases)  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/bac/source/medium.php:26`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-004](#w-004) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** An attacker adds `token=user_token`, the value disclosed in the HTML comment, and requests any `user_id`. The attacker receives that user's profile with no ownership check.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-081](#m-081) — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-082"></a><a id="f-082"></a>
#### F-082 · Unauthenticated user API allows mass assignment

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium - elevated to High as a verified attack-chain keystone in AC-T-002 (Bulk Data Exfiltration via Broken Object Authorization), AC-T-004 (Privilege Escalation via Mass-Assignment on Registration); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases)  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/api/src/UserController.php:225`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-012](#w-012) - Privileged attributes lack an enforced write allowlist

**Issue:** Any caller can send PUT `/vulnerabilities/api/v2/user/2` with `{"name":"x","level":0}`. The controller copies the level field into the user object without any authentication or authorization check, which sets the user to admin level.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◕ [M-082](#m-082) — Allowlist client-controlled fields

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-100"></a><a id="f-100"></a>
#### F-100 · User keeps using a withdrawn right

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium - verified attack-chain keystone in AC-B-006 (User keeps using a withdrawn right); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases)  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/cryptography/source/token_library_impossible.php:103`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** `check_token_impossible` accepts a stateless GCM token and resolves the user from a static array. The token has no expiry, no revocation list and no server-side session, so a withdrawn right or account keeps working. `Token.php`'s `decrypt_token` also does not check 'expires' itself.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-083](#m-083) — Re-check current roles and account status when a session or token is used

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-613](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/613.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-047"></a><a id="f-047"></a>
#### F-047 · Open redirect substring allow check bypassable

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/open_redirect/source/high.php:5`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** An attacker supplies an external URL that contains `info.php` in its query string. The strpos check passes and the victim is redirected off-site.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-051](#m-051) — Use exact-match redirect allowlist

**Classification:** Open Redirect · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-601](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/601.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-048"></a><a id="f-048"></a>
#### F-048 · Open redirect to arbitrary URL

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/open_redirect/source/low.php:4`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** An attacker sends a DVWA link whose redirect parameter names an external site. The server redirects the victim there, which enables phishing.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-052](#m-052) — Map redirect IDs to an allowlist of targets

**Classification:** Open Redirect · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-601](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/601.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-049"></a><a id="f-049"></a>
#### F-049 · Open redirect scheme denylist bypassable

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/open_redirect/source/medium.php:11`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** An attacker uses a protocol-relative URL. The regex only rejects explicit http and https schemes, so the victim is redirected off-site.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-053](#m-053) — Replace scheme denylist with target allowlist

**Classification:** Open Redirect · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-601](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/601.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-057"></a><a id="f-057"></a>
#### F-057 · Request URI reflected unescaped into inline script string

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/api/source/low.php:47`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** An attacker sends a victim a link to the API lab page whose query string contains a single-quote breakout such as '-`alert(document.cookie)`-'. `REQUEST_URI` is inserted inside a JavaScript string literal in the served page, so the injected code runs in the victim's DVWA origin.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◑ [M-061](#m-061) — JSON-encode server values embedded

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-058"></a><a id="f-058"></a>
#### F-058 · Reflected XSS of username on brute-force login

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/brute/source/low.php:21`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** An attacker sends a victim a link whose username parameter contains script. The value is written into \$html without HTML encoding, and `index.php` renders \$html, so the script runs in the victim's DVWA session.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◑ [M-062](#m-062) — HTML-encode username before rendering

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-059"></a><a id="f-059"></a>
#### F-059 · Reflected XSS

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/cryptography/source/low.php:56`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** An attacker sends a victim the link `/vulnerabilities/cryptography/index.php/`">`<script>`...&lt;`/script`&gt;. `PHP_SELF` includes the path info and is written unencoded into the form action attribute, so the script runs in the victim's session.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◑ [M-063](#m-063) — Encode or drop PHP_SELF in form action

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-060"></a><a id="f-060"></a>
#### F-060 · Reflected script injection bypassing permissive CSP

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/csp/source/high.php:10`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** The CSP pages (low, medium, high) write the POST include value into the page body unencoded. Each level's CSP permits attacker-influenced script: low allowlists public CDNs, medium allows unsafe-inline with a static nonce, and high allows the same-origin JSONP endpoint.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◑ [M-064](#m-064) — HTML-encode reflected input and tighten CSP

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-061"></a><a id="f-061"></a>
#### F-061 · Unvalidated JSONP callback reflected into script response

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/csp/source/jsonp.php:12`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** An attacker who can add a script tag to a DVWA CSP challenge page points it at `vulnerabilities/csp/source/jsonp.php?callback`=`alert(document.domain)`;//. The endpoint echoes the callback verbatim before the JSON.

The browser runs the attacker's JavaScript from a same-origin, CSP-allowed source, which defeats a script-src 'self' policy.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - Line 5 takes \$callback from `$_GET['callback']`, and line 12 echoes it unescaped as the start of the script body. The page that would include this script was not read in this job.

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◑ [M-065](#m-065) — Validate JSONP callback against an identifier allowlist

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-062"></a><a id="f-062"></a>
#### F-062 · Reflected XSS of username in test credentials page

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/csrf/test_credentials.php:27`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** The posted username is SQL-escaped but not HTML-encoded, and it is interpolated into the login-state heading. Markup in the username is therefore rendered in the browser.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◑ [M-062](#m-062) — HTML-encode username before rendering

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-063"></a><a id="f-063"></a>
#### F-063 · Reflected XSS

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/view_source.php:85`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** An attacker sends a logged-in victim a `view_source.php` link whose id contains markup. The id is echoed unencoded into the page, so script runs in the victim's DVWA session.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◑ [M-066](#m-066) — HTML-encode id before output

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-064"></a><a id="f-064"></a>
#### F-064 · Reflected cross-site scripting

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/xss_r/index.php:51`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** An attacker sends an authenticated victim a link to `/vulnerabilities/xss_r/?name=``&lt;payload&gt;`. The low level reflects the name parameter unencoded. The script runs in the DVWA origin and can read the non-HttpOnly session cookie.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `low.php:8` appends `$_GET['name']` to \$html without encoding, and `index.php:51` places \$html into the page body that `dvwaHtmlEcho` outputs. `medium.php:8` strips only a literal '&lt;script&gt;'. `high.php:8` strips only a &lt;`s.c.r.i.p.t` pattern. All three levels send X-XSS-Protection: 0.

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◑ [M-067](#m-067) — HTML-encode reflected name with htmlspecialchars

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-065"></a><a id="f-065"></a>
#### F-065 · Reflected name parameter rendered without HTML encoding

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/xss_r/source/low.php:8`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** An attacker sends a victim a link with name=&lt;script&gt;`alert(document.cookie)`&lt;`/script`&gt;. On the low level the value is concatenated raw into the page HTML, and the X-XSS-Protection: 0 header disables the browser filter.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `xss_r/source/low.php:6-8` concatenates `$_GET['name']` into \$html without encoding. The `xss_r` index page that echoes \$html was not served, so the final render is not shown. The impossible level encodes with htmlspecialchars.

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◑ [M-068](#m-068) — HTML-encode the reflected name parameter

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04)

<a id="t-066"></a><a id="f-066"></a>
#### F-066 · Stored XSS payloads accepted into guestbook entries

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - DVWA Web Application  ·  **Location:** `vulnerabilities/xss_s/source/low.php:16`

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: `Low.php` stores input with only SQL escaping, but the `dvwaGuestbook()` output rendering was not served, so whether stored payloads render unencoded is unproven.

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** An authenticated attacker signs the guestbook with a script or event-handler payload. The name field is only SQL-escaped at low, and the medium/high filters are bypassable. The payload is stored and rendered to every visitor by `dvwaGuestbook()`.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous - `low.php:16` stores the message and name after only stripslashes/mysqli_real_escape_string. `medium.php:14` strips only a literal '&lt;script&gt;' and `high.php:14` only a &lt;`s.c.r.i.p.t` pattern from the name. The guestbook renderer `dvwaGuestbook()` (`xss_s/index.php:72`) was not in the served source, so output encoding is unverified.

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◑ [M-003](#m-003) — Manual review: verify Stored XSS payloads accepted into guestbook entries · ◑ [M-069](#m-069) — Encode guestbook fields on output with htmlspecialchars

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-055"></a><a id="f-055"></a>
#### F-055 · Container images referenced by floating tags without digest pinning

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Found by:** survey and scanners

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Instances (2):** `compose.yml`: 29; `Dockerfile`: 1

**Issue:** The images are referenced by tag only, with no @sha256 digest. Nothing verifies their integrity or provenance. The images come from Docker official library repositories, but that does not pin the exact image bytes.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `Dockerfile` line 1 FROM docker.io/library/php:8-apache and line 19 COPY `--from=composer:latest`; `compose.yml` line 12 dvwa:latest with `pull_policy` always (line 14) and line 29 mariadb:10, none pinned by digest.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-059](#m-059) — Pin base and builder images by sha256 digest

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-494](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/494.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-SUPPLY-01](#tc-supply-01) · Rules: IAC-001

<a id="t-056"></a><a id="f-056"></a>
#### F-056 · Third-party GitHub Actions pinned to mutable tags instead of commit SHAs

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (3)

**Found by:** survey and scanners

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-018](#w-018) - Build pipeline trusts mutable third-party references

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-1](#tb-1) - external → CI/CD Pipeline · Authorization: Mutable-tag action runs inside the build job that holds the `GITHUB_TOKEN` used for the `ghcr.io` push across this boundary.

**Instances (3):** `.github/workflows/docker-image.yml`: 16; `.github/workflows/codeql-analysis.yml`: 45; `.github/workflows/shiftleft-analysis.yml`: 24

**Issue:** The workflows reference third-party actions by mutable tags such as @v3 or @main instead of commit SHAs. A compromised publisher can retroactively inject malicious code into a tag that is already in use.

The actions come from well-known publishers, and pushes are limited to master and manual dispatch, but neither control pins the code that runs.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `docker-image.yml` lines 16, 19, 22 reference actions by @v3; `shiftleft-analysis.yml` line 24 references scan-action@v1.3.0 with `GITHUB_TOKEN` at line 27; `codeql-analysis.yml` lines 45, 56, 70 use @v4.33.0 tags.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-060](#m-060) — Pin third-party dependencies to immutable versions

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-829](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/829.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-SUPPLY-01](#tc-supply-01) · Rules: IAC-011

<a id="t-073"></a><a id="f-073"></a>
#### F-073 · Workflows lack explicit permissions block

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `.github/workflows/docker-image.yml:10`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-1](#tb-1) - external → CI/CD Pipeline · Authorization: The boundary assumes the `GITHUB_TOKEN` can write only the project's package, but the build job sets no permissions so default scope applies.

**Issue:** None of the workflows declares permissions at workflow or job level, so each job's `GITHUB_TOKEN` inherits the repository default scope, which on legacy-default repositories is read-write across contents, packages and more. A compromised action or injected step in the build, scan or vulnerable jobs can push commits, create releases or overwrite packages beyond what the job needs.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `docker-image.yml` job build (line 10), `codeql-analysis.yml` job analyze (line 20), `shiftleft-analysis.yml` Scan-Build (line 16), `pytest.yml` and `vulnerable.yml` jobs define no permissions key; effective default scope is set in repository settings, not visible in source.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-075](#m-075) — Add least-privilege permissions blocks to every workflow

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-250](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/250.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-074"></a><a id="f-074"></a>
#### F-074 · Container Runs as Root

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `Dockerfile:1`

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: No USER instruction in `Dockerfile` lines 1-25, but the file continues past the served window, so a later USER cannot be ruled out.

**Issue:** Container Runs as Root: Containers running as root expand container-escape blast radius.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

**Fix:** ◑ [M-006](#m-006) — Manual review: verify Container Runs as Root · ◑ [M-008](#m-008) — `Dockerfile` USER directive

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-250](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/250.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-INFRA-01](#tc-infra-01) · Rules: IAC-002

<a id="t-075"></a><a id="f-075"></a>
#### F-075 · Missing Workflow Permissions Block

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (5)

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-011](#w-011) - Missing Workflow Permissions Block

**Instances (5):** `.github/workflows/codeql-analysis.yml`: 20; `.github/workflows/docker-image.yml`: 10; `.github/workflows/pytest.yml`: 15; `.github/workflows/shiftleft-analysis.yml`: 16; `.github/workflows/vulnerable.yml`: 9

**Issue:** Missing Workflow Permissions Block: Without an explicit permissions block at workflow or job level, the job inherits the repository default (commonly write-all) for `GITHUB_TOKEN` - any compromised step can push code, create releases, or approve PRs.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-009](#m-009) — Apply least-privilege permissions

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-732](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/732.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-CI-01](#tc-ci-01) · Rules: IAC-010

_**Reliability** says how sure this report is that a finding is real, not how severe it is. It is a judgement, not a probability._

<a id="ac-t-005"></a>
### AC-T-005 — Authentication Bypass via Exposed Secret Material

> **Source:** technical abuse case · **Actor:** Anonymous Internet Attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🔴 Critical · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Goal:** Forge trusted tokens / credentials and impersonate any user.

**Prerequisite:** Signing material or other secrets are reachable (committed to a public repo, served by an unauthenticated route, or in an exposed directory).

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟠 [F-029](#f-029) — Hardcoded symmetric encryption keys in source | A private key, signing secret, or credential<br/>file is committed to the source repository<br/>or served without authentication. |
| 2 | _no matching finding_<br/>`vulnerabilities/cryptography/source/token_library_impossible.php:99` | The exposed key/secret is the same one the<br/>server trusts, so a token signed with it (or<br/>the leaked credential) is accepted as<br/>authentic. |

**Scenario impact**

Exposed signing material collapses the entire authentication boundary: any attacker who reads the key can mint a valid token for any identity or role, with no credential ever required.

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ● [M-035](#m-035) — Load keys from a secret store (**P1**): addresses 🟠 [F-029](#f-029) — Hardcoded symmetric encryption keys in source at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

---

<a id="ac-t-002"></a>
### AC-T-002 — Bulk Data Exfiltration via Broken Object Authorization

> **Source:** technical abuse case · **Actor:** Authenticated Internet Attacker - authenticated low-privilege user · **Combined Risk:** 🟡 Medium · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Goal:** Enumerate and exfiltrate other users' records, then escalate own permissions via unguarded mass assignment.

**Prerequisite:** Attacker holds a valid, non-privileged user account.

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟡 [F-081](#f-081) — IDOR guarded by static public token | Attacker enumerates and retrieves records<br/>for arbitrary object IDs; no ownership<br/>comparison is performed. |
| 2 | 🟡 [F-082](#f-082) — Unauthenticated user API allows mass assignment | Update endpoint persists an unfiltered `role`<br/>(or equivalent) field supplied in the<br/>request body. |

**Scenario impact**

The ownership gap exposes every record, and the mass-assignment gap lets the same low-privilege actor self-elevate - together they turn a single compromised account into full tenant data access and role escalation.

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ◑ [M-081](#m-081) — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization (**P3**): addresses 🟡 [F-081](#f-081) — IDOR guarded by static public token at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.
- ◕ [M-082](#m-082) — Allowlist client-controlled fields (**P2**): addresses 🟡 [F-082](#f-082) — Unauthenticated user API allows mass assignment at **Step 2**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

---

<a id="ac-t-004"></a>
### AC-T-004 — Privilege Escalation via Mass-Assignment on Registration

> **Source:** technical abuse case · **Actor:** Anonymous Internet Attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🔴 Critical · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Goal:** Obtain an administrator account without any existing privilege.

**Prerequisite:** Self-registration is open (one unauthenticated POST).

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟡 [F-082](#f-082) — Unauthenticated user API allows mass assignment | The account-creation handler persists the<br/>request body wholesale, so a client-supplied<br/>`role` (or `isAdmin`) field is written verbatim. |

**Scenario impact**

A single unauthenticated request mints an admin account when the registration handler trusts a client-supplied role field - the most direct full-compromise path in role-based apps with open sign-up.

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ◕ [M-082](#m-082) — Allowlist client-controlled fields (**P2**): addresses 🟡 [F-082](#f-082) — Unauthenticated user API allows mass assignment at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

---

<a id="ac-t-006"></a>
### AC-T-006 — Remote Code Execution via Server-Side Injection

> **Source:** technical abuse case · **Actor:** Anonymous Internet Attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🔴 Critical · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Goal:** Execute arbitrary code in the application process.

**Prerequisite:** An input reaches a server-side interpreter / template / eval.

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|----------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟡 [F-076](#f-076) — Help page evals contents | Attacker-controlled input is passed to `eval`,<br/>a server-side template engine, an unsafe<br/>sandbox, or an unsafe deserializer. |

**Scenario impact**

A single injection into a server-side interpreter yields code execution in the application process - the highest-impact outcome, granting full filesystem and network access from one unauthenticated request.

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ◕ [M-076](#m-076) — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input (**P2**): addresses 🟡 [F-076](#f-076) — Help page evals contents at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

---

<a id="ac-b-006"></a>
### AC-B-006 — User keeps using a withdrawn right

> **Source:** business abuse case · **Actor:** attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🟡 Medium · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|--------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟡 [F-100](#f-100) — User keeps using a withdrawn right | Check whether a withdrawn role, permission,<br/>or account keeps working through an existing<br/>session, token, or cached authorization. |

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ◑ [M-083](#m-083) — Re-check current roles and account status when a session or token is used (**P3**): addresses 🟡 [F-100](#f-100) — User keeps using a withdrawn right at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

## 10. Mitigation Register

Changes are ordered by priority: P1 before deployment, P2 this sprint, P3 next quarter, P4 backlog. Each entry links its findings and gives the available implementation steps and checks.

### P1 — Immediate

<a id="m-013"></a>
#### M-013 — Validate target host and use escapeshellarg

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-004](#f-004) — OS command injection in health connectivity check and ping feature

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-001](#w-001)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-78](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/78.html)

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/api/src/HealthController.php:88`

**How:**

1. Validate the target and ip values with `filter_var(FILTER_VALIDATE_IP)`, or with a strict hostname regex for target, and reject anything that fails.
2. Replace the `str_replace` metacharacter blacklist in `medium.php` with that validation.
3. Pass the value through `escapeshellarg()`, or better, avoid the shell entirely by running ping through `proc_open` with an argument array.
4. Require an authenticated, authorized token on health/connectivity, or remove the endpoint.

**Verification:** Run curl -X POST -d '{"target":"`127.0.0.1`;sleep 10"}' `http://host/vulnerabilities/api/v2/health/connectivity` and confirm that it returns immediately with a validation error. Then run an automated test that submits values containing shell metacharacters to the exec ping feature at the low and medium levels. Assert that they are rejected with an invalid-IP error and never reach `shell_exec`.

**Reference:** [CWE-78: Improper Neutralization of Special Elements used in an OS Command (OS Command Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/78.html)

---

<a id="m-014"></a>
#### M-014 — Use prepared statements for user update query

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-005](#f-005) — SQL injection in user detail update endpoint

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-002](#w-002)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) - SQL Injection

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/authbypass/change_user_details.php:47`

**How:**

1. Replace the concatenated query with `mysqli_prepare('UPDATE users SET first_name=?, last_name=? WHERE user_id=?')` and bind the values with types 'ssi'.
2. Cast id with intval and reject non-string name fields.

**Verification:** Send `first_name`=`"x'`, password='a' -- " and confirm the password column for user 1 is unchanged and the literal string is stored instead.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: SQL Injection Prevention Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/SQL_Injection_Prevention_Cheat_Sheet.html)

---

<a id="m-023"></a>
#### M-023 — Bind log values with prepared statements

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-016](#f-016) — SQL injection

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-002](#w-002)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) - SQL Injection

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/bac/source/low.php:81`

**How:**

1. Use `mysqli_prepare` with bound parameters for the `bac_log` INSERT, as `impossible.php` does.
2. Take the client IP from `REMOTE_ADDR` unless the request comes from a trusted proxy.

**Verification:** Send X-Forwarded-For: 1',`SLEEP(5)`)-- and confirm there is no delay and the row contains the literal value.

**Reference:** [CWE-89: Improper Neutralization of Special Elements used in an SQL Command (SQL Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html)

---

<a id="m-024"></a>
#### M-024 — Use prepared statements for login lookup

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-017](#f-017) — SQL injection in brute-force login query

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-002](#w-002)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) - SQL Injection

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/brute/source/low.php:13`

**How:**

1. Replace the string-built query with `mysqli_prepare` and bind username and password as parameters.
2. Remove the `die()` that echoes `mysqli_error`.

**Verification:** Request ?`username=admin`'--+&`password=x`&Login=Login and confirm the login fails and no SQL error is returned.

**Reference:** [CWE-89: Improper Neutralization of Special Elements used in an SQL Command (SQL Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html)

---

<a id="m-025"></a>
#### M-025 — Bind session id via prepared statement

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-018](#f-018) — Second-order SQL injection

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-002](#w-002)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) - SQL Injection

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/sqli/source/high.php:11`

**How:**

1. Validate the id as an integer when it is stored in the session.
2. Use a parameterised query in `high.php`.

**Verification:** Store a non-numeric id through `session-input.php` and confirm that it is rejected.

**Reference:** [CWE-89: Improper Neutralization of Special Elements used in an SQL Command (SQL Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html)

---

<a id="m-026"></a>
#### M-026 — Use prepared statements with bound integer id

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-019](#f-019) — SQL injection

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-002](#w-002)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) - SQL Injection

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/sqli/source/low.php:11`

**How:**

1. Validate that id is numeric and cast it with intval.
2. Use a prepared statement that binds id as an integer, as `impossible.php` does.

**Verification:** Run an automated SQLi scanner against the id parameter and confirm that it reports no injectable parameter.

**Reference:** [CWE-89: Improper Neutralization of Special Elements used in an SQL Command (SQL Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html)

---

<a id="m-027"></a>
#### M-027 — Bind id as integer parameter in prepared query

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-020](#f-020) — SQL injection in unquoted numeric context despite escaping

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-002](#w-002)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) - SQL Injection

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/sqli/source/medium.php:12`

**How:**

1. Reject non-numeric id values and cast them with intval.
2. Use a prepared statement with an integer-bound parameter.

**Verification:** Run an automated SQLi scanner against the POST id and confirm that no injection is detected.

**Reference:** [CWE-89: Improper Neutralization of Special Elements used in an SQL Command (SQL Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html)

---

<a id="m-028"></a>
#### M-028 — Parameterize query and validate cookie id

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-021](#f-021) — Blind SQL injection

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-002](#w-002)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) - SQL Injection

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/sqli_blind/source/high.php:13`

**How:**

1. Validate the cookie id as numeric.
2. Execute the lookup through a prepared statement.

**Verification:** Run a blind-SQLi scanner against the id cookie and confirm that no injection is detected.

**Reference:** [CWE-89: Improper Neutralization of Special Elements used in an SQL Command (SQL Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html)

---

<a id="m-029"></a>
#### M-029 — Use parameterized query for blind lookup

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-022](#f-022) — Blind SQL injection

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-002](#w-002)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) - SQL Injection

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/sqli_blind/source/low.php:13`

**How:**

1. Validate id as numeric.
2. Use a prepared statement with a bound integer.

**Verification:** Run a blind-SQLi scanner against id and confirm that no injection is detected.

**Reference:** [CWE-89: Improper Neutralization of Special Elements used in an SQL Command (SQL Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html)

---

<a id="m-030"></a>
#### M-030 — Bind id as integer in prepared statement

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-023](#f-023) — Blind SQL injection

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-002](#w-002)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) - SQL Injection

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/sqli_blind/source/medium.php:15`

**How:**

1. Cast and validate id as an integer.
2. Use a prepared statement.

**Verification:** Run a blind-SQLi scanner against the POST id and confirm that no injection is detected.

**Reference:** [CWE-89: Improper Neutralization of Special Elements used in an SQL Command (SQL Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html)

<a id="m-031"></a>
#### M-031 — Require authentication on every exposed endpoint

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-024](#f-024) — Unauthenticated database reset in setup page

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-306](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/306.html)

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `setup.php:6`

**How:**

1. Require an authenticated admin session (`dvwaPageStartup(array('authenticated'))` plus role check) once the database exists.
2. Disable `setup.php` after initial installation via a config flag.

**Verification:** Without a session, GET `setup.php` then POST `create_db` with the token; expect redirect to login and no DROP DATABASE.

**Reference:** [CWE-306: Missing Authentication for Critical Function](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/306.html)

---

<a id="m-035"></a>
#### M-035 — Load keys from a secret store

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-029](#f-029) — Hardcoded symmetric encryption keys in source

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-007](#w-007)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-321](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html) - Use of Hard-coded Cryptographic Key

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `vulnerabilities/cryptography/source/token_library_impossible.php:3`

**How:**

1. Load a randomly generated 32-byte key from the environment or a secret manager.
2. Use a distinct key for each purpose, and rotate the exposed keys.

**Verification:** grep the repository for the old key literals and confirm none remain.

**Reference:** [CWE-321: Use of Hard-coded Cryptographic Key](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html)

### P2 — This Sprint

<a id="m-007"></a>
#### M-007 — docker-compose secrets supplied by reference

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-027](#f-027) — Hardcoded MariaDB root and application passwords in compose

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-007](#w-007)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) - Use of Hard-coded Credentials

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `compose.yml:31`

**How:** Move the value to Compose `secrets` or an untracked env file and reference it as `${VAR}`; rotate the committed value - A credential written as a literal in a service's `environment` is readable by everyone with repository access and runs unchanged wherever the file is deployed.

1. Replace the literal `MYSQL_ROOT_PASSWORD` and `MYSQL_PASSWORD` values with `MYSQL_ROOT_PASSWORD_FILE` and `MYSQL_PASSWORD_FILE` that point to Docker secrets, or with variables from an untracked .env file excluded from version control.
2. Generate strong, random, unique passwords for each deployment.
3. Rotate the credentials that have already been exposed.

**Verification:** Run grep -nE '`MYSQL_(ROOT_)?PASSWORD=`' `compose.yml` and confirm that it returns no literal values. Then confirm that the database starts using the secret files.

**Reference:** [CWE-798: Use of Hard-coded Credentials](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html)

---

<a id="m-010"></a>
#### M-010 — Require CSRF token and current password

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-001](#f-001) — Missing Anti-CSRF Token on State Changes

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) - Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/captcha/source/low.php:45`

**How:**

1. Call `checkToken` on every password-change POST, including in `high.php`.
2. Require and verify the current password before running the UPDATE.

**Verification:** Submit a cross-origin POST without `user_token` and confirm the password is unchanged.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: Cross Site Request Forgery Prevention Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Cross-Site_Request_Forgery_Prevention_Cheat_Sheet.html)

---

<a id="m-011"></a>
#### M-011 — Use modern cryptographic hash and KDF algorithms

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-002](#f-002) — Broken MD5 hashing used for stored passwords without salt or strength policy
- 🟡 [F-070](#f-070) — Broken hash primitive

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-008](#w-008)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-328](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/328.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `dvwa/includes/dvwaPage.inc.php:651`

**How:**

1. Replace `md5()` with `password_hash($pass, PASSWORD_ARGON2ID, or PASSWORD_BCRYPT with cost >= 12)`.
2. Verify passwords with `password_verify` instead of comparing hashes in SQL.
3. Widen the `users.password` column to at least `VARCHAR(255)`, and rehash on next login with `password_needs_rehash`.
4. Add a server-side minimum length of at least 12 and a breached-password check to all password set and change paths.
5. Use `password_hash()` (bcrypt/argon2) for credentials and `hash('sha256', …)` for integrity.
6. Add a regression test (or CI check) that fails on the vulnerable pattern and passes once the fix is in place.

**Verification:** Run setup and change a password. Then run `SELECT password FROM users` and assert that each value starts with \$2y\$ or \$argon2id\$. Confirm that a change to 'abc' is rejected.

---

<a id="m-015"></a>
#### M-015 — Verify the current password before any password change

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-006](#f-006) — Password change does not require the current password

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-009](#w-009)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-620](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/620.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/csrf/source/low.php:9`

**How:**

1. Require `password_current` in a POST body and verify it against the stored hash before updating.
2. Accept the change only by POST with an anti-CSRF token, and invalidate other sessions after a change.

**Verification:** Send a GET to the change handler with matching new passwords and no current password, then assert that the password is unchanged and the login with the old password still succeeds.

---

<a id="m-016"></a>
#### M-016 — Use authenticated encryption with uniform errors

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-007](#f-007) — Unauthenticated CBC token exposes padding oracle

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-649](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/649.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `vulnerabilities/cryptography/source/token_library_high.php:26`

**How:**

1. Switch to AES-GCM with a random IV, as `token_library_impossible.php` does, or add an HMAC that is verified before decryption.
2. Return one generic error for every validation failure.

**Verification:** Run an automated test asserting that tokens with a modified IV are rejected with the same status as malformed tokens.

**Reference:** [CWE-649](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/649.html)

---

<a id="m-017"></a>
#### M-017 — Force unique admin password

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-008](#f-008) — Default seeded administrator credentials

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-1392](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1392.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `dvwa/includes/DBMS/MySQL.php:55`

**How:**

1. Generate or require an operator-supplied admin password during setup instead of seeding 'password'.
2. Remove the displayed default credentials from `setup.php` and force a password change on first login.

**Verification:** After setup, POST `login.php` with `username=admin`&`password=password` and a valid `user_token`; expect 'Login failed'.

**Reference:** [CWE-1392](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1392.html)
