<a id="m-018"></a>
#### M-018 — Remove default passwords and force setting on first use

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-009](#f-009) — Setup seeds accounts with well-known weak default passwords

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-1393](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1393.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `dvwa/includes/DBMS/MySQL.php:55`

**How:**

1. Generate random initial passwords during setup, or require the operator to set them, instead of fixed literals.
2. Force a password change on first login for any seeded account.

**Verification:** After setup, POST to `login.php` with admin/password and assert the response is 'Login failed'.

<a id="m-019"></a>
#### M-019 — Render user fields via textContent and DOM APIs

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-011](#f-011) — Stored XSS

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-006](#w-006)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) - Cross-site Scripting

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/authbypass/authbypass.js:45`

**How:**

1. In `authbypass.js`, create the input elements with `document.createElement` and set their value property instead of concatenating HTML into `innerHTML`.
2. Encode user name fields with htmlspecialchars server-side at every security level, or validate them on update.

**Verification:** Through `change_user_details`, store a `first_name` containing ">&lt;img `src=x` `onerror=alert`(1)&gt;. Load the authbypass page as admin and confirm that no alert fires and the literal text appears in the input.

**Reference:** [CWE-79: Improper Neutralization of Input During Web Page Generation (XSS)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html)

---

<a id="m-020"></a>
#### M-020 — HTML-escape log fields when rendering access log

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-012](#f-012) — Stored XSS

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-006](#w-006)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) - Cross-site Scripting

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/bac/index.php:71`

**How:**

1. Wrap every log field rendered in `index.php` lines 68-72 with `htmlspecialchars($v, ENT_QUOTES, 'UTF-8')`.
2. Validate the stored IP with `filter_var(FILTER_VALIDATE_IP)` before insert.

**Verification:** Send X-Forwarded-For: &lt;svg `onload=alert`(1)&gt;, reload the BAC page, and confirm the literal text is shown and no script executes.

**Reference:** [CWE-79: Improper Neutralization of Input During Web Page Generation (XSS)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html)

<a id="m-021"></a>
#### M-021 — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer (F-013)

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-013](#f-013) — DOM-based XSS from URL default parameter written

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-006](#w-006)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) - Cross-site Scripting

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/xss_d/index.php:53`

**How:**

1. Parse the parameter with URLSearchParams and check it on the client against a fixed language allowlist before using it.
2. Create option elements with `document.createElement` and set value and `textContent` instead of using `document.write`.

**Verification:** At each level, load `xss_d/?default`=&lt;script&gt;`alert(1)`&lt;`/script`&gt; and ?`default=English`#default=&lt;`/option`&gt;&lt;img `src=x` `onerror=alert`(1)&gt;. Confirm that no script runs and that the option text shows the literal string.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: DOM Based XSS Prevention Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/DOM_based_XSS_Prevention_Cheat_Sheet.html)

<a id="m-022"></a>
#### M-022 — Use prepared statements

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-014](#f-014) — SQL injection
- 🟠 [F-015](#f-015) — SQL injection

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-002](#w-002)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) - SQL Injection

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `dvwa/includes/DBMS/MySQL.php:37`

**How:** Use prepared statements (PDO `bindParam` / mysqli placeholders / Eloquent bindings).

1. Add a regression test (or CI check) that fails on the vulnerable pattern and passes once the fix is in place.

<a id="m-032"></a>
#### M-032 — Replace Referer substring check with anti-CSRF token

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-025](#f-025) — CSRF check bypass

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) - Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/csrf/source/medium.php:5`

**How:**

1. Use synchronizer tokens validated with `checkToken`.
2. If origin checks are kept, parse the `Origin` header and compare the host exactly.

**Verification:** Run an automated test asserting that a request whose Referer host merely contains the server name is rejected.

**Reference:** [CWE-352: Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html)

<a id="m-033"></a>
#### M-033 — Remove secret dumping step from vulnerable workflow

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-026](#f-026) — All repository secrets dumped base64-encoded to workflow

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-014](#w-014)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-532](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/532.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `.github/workflows/vulnerable.yml:54`

**How:**

1. Delete the `get_secrets` job from `.github/workflows/vulnerable.yml`, or the whole workflow if it serves only as a demo.
2. Never pass `toJSON(secrets)` into a step; reference only the individual secrets a step needs.
3. Rotate `DVWA_SECRET_KEY` and any other repository secrets that have been exposed in historical run logs, and delete those logs.

**Verification:** Run `grep -rn "toJSON(secrets)\|base64" .github/workflows/` and confirm no match remains in any step that handles secrets; inspect the next master run log for absence of encoded output.

**Reference:** [CWE-532: Insertion of Sensitive Information into Log File](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/532.html)

<a id="m-034"></a>
#### M-034 — Constrain file paths to a safe base directory

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-028](#f-028) — Path traversal in source viewer discloses

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-003](#w-003)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-22](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/22.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/view_source.php:63`

**How:**

1. Accept id only from the known module list and security only from low, medium, high and impossible, and return Not found for any other value before calling `file_get_contents`.
2. Resolve the final path with realpath and verify that it lies under vulnerabilities/`&lt;id&gt;``/source/`.

**Verification:** Request `view_source.php`?`id=fi`&`security=`../../..`/config/config.inc` and confirm that the response is Not found with no file contents.

**Reference:** [CWE-22: Path Traversal](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/22.html)

<a id="m-036"></a>
#### M-036 — Hash passwords with a strong, salted algorithm

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-030](#f-030) — Unsalted MD5 password hashing

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-008](#w-008)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-916](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/916.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `vulnerabilities/brute/source/low.php:9`

**How:**

1. Store passwords with `password_hash(PASSWORD_DEFAULT)` and verify them with `password_verify`.
2. Migrate existing `MD5` hashes when each user next logs in.

**Verification:** Inspect the users table after a password change and confirm the stored value has the \$2y\$ or \$argon2 prefix.

<a id="m-037"></a>
#### M-037 — Restrict included files to a fixed allowlist

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-032](#f-032) — Unvalidated page parameter passed to PHP include

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-98](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/98.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/fi/index.php:36`

**How:**

1. Apply the `impossible.php` allowlist (`in_array` with strict comparison) at every security level, or map the page parameter to a fixed array of allowed files and include only entries from that map.
2. Reject values that contain path separators or stream wrappers, and confirm that the realpath lies inside the fi directory.
3. Set `allow_url_include=Off` and restrict `open_basedir` to the application directory.

**Verification:** Request ?`page=`../../../..`/etc/passwd` and ?page=`http://example.com/x.txt` at each level. Confirm that the response is 'File not found' and contains no file contents such as root:x:0:0.

**Reference:** [CWE-98](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/98.html)

<a id="m-038"></a>
#### M-038 — Allowlist exact include filenames

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-033](#f-033) — File inclusion
- 🟠 [F-034](#f-034) — File inclusion

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-98](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/98.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/fi/source/high.php:7`

**How:**

1. Compare the page parameter against an exact list of allowed filenames.
2. Reject values that contain a scheme or path separator.
3. Map the page parameter to a fixed array of allowed filenames.
4. Disable `allow_url_include` in the PHP configuration.

**Verification:** Run an automated test asserting that only `file1.php` through `file3.php` and `include.php` are accepted.

**Reference:** [CWE-98](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/98.html)

<a id="m-039"></a>
#### M-039 — Allowlist image types and randomize stored names

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-035](#f-035) — Unrestricted file upload stores any file under its client name

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-434](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/434.html) - Unrestricted Upload of File with Dangerous Type

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `vulnerabilities/upload/source/low.php:9`

**How:**

1. Allow only image extensions, verify the content server-side, and re-encode the image as `impossible.php` does.
2. Store uploads under randomly generated file names.
3. Store files outside the webroot, or disable script execution in the uploads directory.

**Verification:** Upload a .php file at the low level and confirm that it is rejected and that no file appears in hackable/uploads.

**Reference:** [CWE-434: Unrestricted Upload of File with Dangerous Type](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/434.html)

<a id="m-040"></a>
#### M-040 — Validate extension and content server-side

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-036](#f-036) — Upload type check trusts client-supplied MIME type

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-434](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/434.html) - Unrestricted Upload of File with Dangerous Type

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `vulnerabilities/upload/source/medium.php:14`

**How:**

1. Ignore the client-declared MIME type and check the file extension against an allowlist.
2. Detect the type server-side with finfo or getimagesize to verify the image content.
3. Re-encode images and store them under random names with a fixed safe extension.

**Verification:** Upload a .php file declared as image/jpeg and confirm that it is rejected.

#### M-041 — Use authenticated encryption

- [CWE-649](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/649.html)

**How:**

1. Encrypt tokens with AES-256-GCM using a random IV per token, or sign them with an HMAC.
2. Reject tokens that fail tag verification before parsing the JSON.

<a id="m-042"></a>

**Addresses:**

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-649](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/649.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `vulnerabilities/cryptography/source/token_library_high.php:126`

**How:**

1. Replace AES-CBC with AES-GCM, or encrypt-then-MAC, and verify the tag before decryption.
2. Return one generic error for every token failure and drop the exception message from 'extra'.

<a id="m-043"></a>

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) - Missing Authorization

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/authbypass/change_user_details.php:11`

**How:**

1. Call `dvwaPageStartup(array('authenticated'))` and require `dvwaCurrentUser()`==='admin' on both endpoints, independent of security level.
2. Keep the security level in the server session rather than trusting the security cookie in `dvwaSecurityLevelGet`.

**Verification:** Without a session and with Cookie `security=low`, POST to `change_user_details.php` and GET `get_user_data.php`. Both must return Access denied with HTTP 403.

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-94](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/94.html) - Code Injection

3. Render the help file with include of the validated path, or as static HTML, instead of eval.

<a id="m-008"></a>
#### M-008 — Dockerfile USER directive

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-074](#f-074) — Container Runs as Root

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-250](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/250.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `Dockerfile:1`

**How:** Add USER `<non-root-uid>` - Containers running as root expand container-escape blast radius.

1. Add USER `<non-root-uid>`
2. Add a regression test (or CI check) that fails on the vulnerable pattern and passes once the fix is in place.

**Verification:** Re-run the IAC-002 scanner check; it reports no match at `Dockerfile:1`, and the regression test passes.

---

<a id="m-009"></a>
#### M-009 — Apply least-privilege permissions

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-075](#f-075) — Missing Workflow Permissions Block

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-011](#w-011)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-732](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/732.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `.github/workflows/codeql-analysis.yml:20`

**How:** Add `permissions: { contents: read }` at workflow root; elevate per-job only where needed - Without an explicit permissions block at workflow or job level, the job inherits the repository default (commonly write-all) for `GITHUB_TOKEN` - any compromised step can push code, create releases, or approve PRs.

1. Add `permissions: { contents: read }` at workflow root; elevate per-job only where needed
2. Add a regression test (or CI check) that fails on the vulnerable pattern and passes once the fix is in place.

**Verification:** Re-run the IAC-010 scanner check; it reports no match at `.github/workflows/codeql-analysis.yml:20`, and the regression test passes.

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-003](#f-003) — Raw Database Error Disclosure to Client

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-209](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/209.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/brute/source/low.php:13`

**How:**

1. Replace `die(mysqli_error(...))` with a generic message and write the details to a server-side log.
2. Disable `display_errors` in production.

<a id="m-044"></a>
#### M-044 — Use Docker secrets with strong random DB passwords

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-040](#f-040) — Hardcoded weak MariaDB root and application credentials

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-007](#w-007)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) - Use of Hard-coded Credentials

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `compose.yml:31`

**How:**

1. Replace the `MYSQL_ROOT_PASSWORD` and `MYSQL_PASSWORD` literals with `MYSQL_ROOT_PASSWORD_FILE` and `MYSQL_PASSWORD_FILE` pointing at Docker secrets generated per deployment.
2. Or set `MARIADB_RANDOM_ROOT_PASSWORD`=yes so no static root password exists, and pass the app password to the web container through the same secret.

**Verification:** Run `docker compose config` and confirm it shows no plaintext password values. Then confirm `mysql -uroot -pdvwa -h db` from the web container fails.

**Reference:** [CWE-798: Use of Hard-coded Credentials](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html)

<a id="m-045"></a>
#### M-045 — Add login rate limiting and lockout

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-041](#f-041) — Login lacks attempt throttling or lockout

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-307](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/307.html) - Improper Restriction of Excessive Authentication Attempts

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `login.php:41`

**How:**

1. Track `failed_login` per user and per source IP and reject attempts beyond a threshold with increasing delay.
2. Reset counters on successful login and log lockout events.

**Verification:** Script 20 failed logins for admin and confirm subsequent correct login is rejected during lockout.

**Reference:** [CWE-307: Improper Restriction of Excessive Authentication Attempts](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/307.html)

<a id="m-046"></a>

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-307](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/307.html) - Improper Restriction of Excessive Authentication Attempts

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `vulnerabilities/brute/source/low.php:12`

**How:**

1. Track failed attempts per account and per client IP, and apply progressive delays or a CAPTCHA after a threshold.
2. Move credentials to POST and log failed attempts.

**Verification:** Script 50 bad logins and confirm that later attempts are rejected or throttled.

**Reference:** [CWE-307: Improper Restriction of Excessive Authentication Attempts](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/307.html)

2. Alternatively, record CAPTCHA success in \$_SESSION and require it in step 2.

<a id="m-058"></a>
#### M-058 — Require a caller-chosen password hashed with password_hash

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-054](#f-054) — API-created users all receive the same default password

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-1393](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1393.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/api/src/UserController.php:167`

**How:**

1. Require a password field in the add-user input and validate its strength.
2. Store `password_hash()` of the supplied password instead of a constant `SHA-256` value.

<a id="m-059"></a>
#### M-059 — Pin base and builder images by sha256 digest

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-055](#f-055) — Container images referenced by floating tags without digest pinning

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-494](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/494.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `Dockerfile:1`

**How:**

1. Reference the php, composer and mariadb images as name:tag@sha256:`<digest>` in `Dockerfile` and `compose.yml`, for example docker.io/library/mariadb:10.11.x@sha256:`<digest>`.
2. Update the digests only through reviewed Renovate or Dependabot pull requests.
3. Sign and attest the pushed dvwa image and verify the signature before deployment.

**Verification:** Run grep -nE '^FROM|`--from=|image:'` `Dockerfile` `compose.yml` and confirm that every reference, including the `compose.yml` db image, contains @sha256:.

**Reference:** [CWE-494](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/494.html)

---

<a id="m-060"></a>
#### M-060 — Pin third-party dependencies to immutable versions

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-056](#f-056) — Third-party GitHub Actions pinned to mutable tags instead of commit SHAs

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-018](#w-018)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-829](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/829.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `.github/workflows/docker-image.yml:16`

**How:**

1. Replace every uses: reference in .github/workflows with the full 40-character commit SHA, and keep the version in a trailing comment.
2. Enable Dependabot or Renovate for the github-actions ecosystem so SHA pins are updated through reviewed pull requests.
3. Do not pass `GITHUB_TOKEN` to third-party actions such as scan-action unless it is required.
4. Scope `GITHUB_TOKEN` with a permissions block.

**Verification:** Run grep -`rnE` 'uses: [^ ]+@' .github/workflows/ | grep -vE '@[0-9a-f]{40}' and confirm that it returns no lines.

**Reference:** [GitHub Docs: Security Hardening For Github Actions](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/security-guides/security-hardening-for-github-actions#using-third-party-actions)

---

<a id="m-061"></a>
#### M-061 — JSON-encode server values embedded

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-057](#f-057) — Request URI reflected unescaped into inline script string

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-006](#w-006)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) - Cross-site Scripting

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/api/source/low.php:47`

**How:**

1. Build the base URL from a server-side constant instead of `REQUEST_URI`.
2. If a dynamic value is needed, emit it with `json_encode($v, JSON_HEX_TAG|JSON_HEX_APOS|JSON_HEX_QUOT)`.

**Verification:** Request the page with ?x=';`alert(1)`;// and confirm the rendered script contains no unescaped quote.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: Cross Site Scripting Prevention Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Cross_Site_Scripting_Prevention_Cheat_Sheet.html)

<a id="m-062"></a>
#### M-062 — HTML-encode username before rendering

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-058](#f-058) — Reflected XSS of username on brute-force login
- 🟡 [F-062](#f-062) — Reflected XSS of username in test credentials page

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-006](#w-006)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) - Cross-site Scripting

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/brute/source/low.php:21`

**How:**

1. Wrap \$user in `htmlspecialchars($user, ENT_QUOTES, 'UTF-8')` at every level.
2. Encode the avatar value before placing it in the img src attribute.
3. Wrap \$user in htmlspecialchars before interpolating it.
4. Use prepared statements for the query, and keep display encoding separate from SQL escaping.

**Verification:** Submit a username containing &lt;script&gt;`alert(1)`&lt;`/script`&gt; and confirm the response contains the encoded entities.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: Cross Site Scripting Prevention Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Cross_Site_Scripting_Prevention_Cheat_Sheet.html)

<a id="m-064"></a>
#### M-064 — HTML-encode reflected input and tighten CSP

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-060](#f-060) — Reflected script injection bypassing permissive CSP

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-006](#w-006)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) - Cross-site Scripting

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `vulnerabilities/csp/source/high.php:10`

**How:**

1. Encode reflected input with htmlspecialchars before adding it to the page.
2. Remove the CDN allowlist and the static nonce, use per-response random nonces, and hardcode the JSONP callback.

<a id="m-065"></a>
#### M-065 — Validate JSONP callback against an identifier allowlist

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-061](#f-061) — Unvalidated JSONP callback reflected into script response

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-006](#w-006)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) - Cross-site Scripting

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/csp/source/jsonp.php:12`

**How:**

1. Use a fixed callback name, as `jsonp_impossible.php` does, or reject any callback that does not match `^[A-Za-z_$][A-Za-z0-9_$]{0,63}$`.
2. Send `X-Content-Type-Options`: nosniff and move clients to `fetch()` with plain JSON instead of JSONP.

**Verification:** Request `jsonp.php`?`callback=alert`(1);// and confirm the server rejects it or returns the fixed callback name.

**Reference:** [CWE-79: Improper Neutralization of Input During Web Page Generation (XSS)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html)

<a id="m-067"></a>
#### M-067 — HTML-encode reflected name with htmlspecialchars

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-064](#f-064) — Reflected cross-site scripting

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-006](#w-006)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) - Cross-site Scripting

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/xss_r/index.php:51`

**How:**

1. Encode the name with `htmlspecialchars($name, ENT_QUOTES, 'UTF-8')` before appending it to \$html at every level.
2. Remove the X-XSS-Protection: 0 header and add a restrictive `Content-Security-Policy`.

**Verification:** Request ?name=&lt;img `src=x` `onerror=alert`(1)&gt; at each level and confirm the response contains &lt;img and no script executes.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: Cross Site Scripting Prevention Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Cross_Site_Scripting_Prevention_Cheat_Sheet.html)

---

<a id="m-068"></a>
#### M-068 — HTML-encode the reflected name parameter

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-065](#f-065) — Reflected name parameter rendered without HTML encoding

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-006](#w-006)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) - Cross-site Scripting

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/xss_r/source/low.php:8`

**How:**

1. Apply `htmlspecialchars($_GET['name'], ENT_QUOTES, 'UTF-8')` before concatenating it.
2. Remove the X-XSS-Protection: 0 header and add a restrictive `Content-Security-Policy`.

**Verification:** Request ?name=&lt;script&gt;`alert(1)`&lt;`/script`&gt; on the low level. The response must contain &lt;script&gt;.

---

<a id="m-069"></a>
#### M-069 — Encode guestbook fields on output with htmlspecialchars

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-066](#f-066) — Stored XSS payloads accepted into guestbook entries

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-006](#w-006)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) - Cross-site Scripting

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/xss_s/source/low.php:16`

**How:**

1. In `dvwaGuestbook()`, HTML-encode the name and comment with `htmlspecialchars(..., ENT_QUOTES, 'UTF-8')` when rendering.
2. Replace the blacklist filters on name with encoding, and store the raw value through prepared statements.

**Verification:** Sign the guestbook with name &lt;img `src=x` `onerror=alert`(1)&gt; at each level and confirm it renders as literal text.

**Reference:** [CWE-79: Improper Neutralization of Input During Web Page Generation (XSS)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html)

---

<a id="m-070"></a>
#### M-070 — Log REMOTE_ADDR unless proxy is trusted

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-067](#f-067) — Access log trusts client X-Forwarded-For as source IP

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-348](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/348.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/bac/source/high.php:92`

**How:**

1. Use `REMOTE_ADDR` by default, and honour X-Forwarded-For only when `REMOTE_ADDR` is in a configured trusted-proxy list.
2. Record the raw forwarded header separately, flagged as untrusted.

**Verification:** Send X-Forwarded-For: `1.2.3.4` directly to the app and confirm `bac_log.ip_address` stores the real client address.

**Reference:** [CWE-348](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/348.html)

---

<a id="m-071"></a>
#### M-071 — Stop exposing internal information to clients

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-068](#f-068) — Legacy v1 user API discloses password hashes

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-200](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html) - Exposure of Sensitive Information

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/api/src/User.php:45`

**How:**

1. Remove the password field from every `toArray` branch and reject unsupported API versions in `public/index.php`.
2. Require authentication for user listing endpoints.

**Verification:** Run curl `http://host/vulnerabilities/api/v1/user/` and confirm the response contains no password key, or that it returns 404.

**Reference:** [CWE-200: Exposure of Sensitive Information to an Unauthorized Actor](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html)

---

<a id="m-072"></a>
#### M-072 — Replace the weak cryptographic algorithm

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-069](#f-069) — Repeating-key XOR used as message encryption

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-008](#w-008)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-327](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/327.html) - Use of a Broken or Risky Cryptographic Algorithm

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `vulnerabilities/cryptography/source/low.php:35`

**How:**

1. Use `sodium_crypto_secretbox` or AES-256-GCM in place of `xor_this`.
2. Keep the key outside the source.

**Verification:** Encrypt the same message twice and confirm the ciphertexts differ and do not reveal the key when XORed with the plaintext.

**Reference:** [CWE-327: Use of a Broken or Risky Cryptographic Algorithm](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/327.html)

---

<a id="m-073"></a>
#### M-073 — Remove config backup from web root

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-071](#f-071) — Config backup with DB credentials copied into web root

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-530](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/530.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `dvwa/includes/DBMS/MySQL.php:138`

**How:**

1. Delete the `copy()` of `config.inc.php` to .bak in setup.
2. Deny HTTP access to the config directory in Apache configuration.

**Verification:** After setup, GET `/config/config.inc.php.bak` and expect 403 or 404.

**Reference:** [CWE-530](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/530.html)

---

<a id="m-074"></a>
#### M-074 — Throttle by client and avoid hard per-user lockout

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-072](#f-072) — Attacker-triggerable account lockout

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-645](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/645.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `vulnerabilities/brute/source/impossible.php:88`

**How:**

1. Throttle failed attempts by source IP or device fingerprint as well as by username.
2. Replace the hard lockout with progressive delays or a CAPTCHA challenge.

**Verification:** Send three failed logins for a user from one client and confirm the legitimate user can still log in from another client.

**Reference:** [CWE-645](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/645.html)

---

<a id="m-075"></a>
#### M-075 — Add least-privilege permissions blocks to every workflow

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-073](#f-073) — Workflows lack explicit permissions block for GITHUB_TOKEN

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-250](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/250.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `.github/workflows/docker-image.yml:10`

**How:**

1. Add top-level `permissions: {}` or `contents: read` to every workflow file.
2. Grant per-job only what is needed: packages: write for the docker build job, `security-events: write` for CodeQL and SARIF upload jobs.
3. Set the repository default workflow permission to read-only in repository settings.

**Verification:** Run `grep -L '^permissions:' .github/workflows/*.yml` and confirm it lists no files; check a run's '`GITHUB_TOKEN` Permissions' section shows only the granted scopes.

**Reference:** [GitHub Docs: Automatic Token Authentication](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/security-guides/automatic-token-authentication#modifying-the-permissions-for-the-github_token)

---

<a id="m-077"></a>
#### M-077 — Allowlist help module ids and include safely

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-077](#f-077) — Eval of help file chosen by request path

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-98](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/98.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/view_help.php:20`

**How:**

1. Validate id against the list of known modules and locale against known locales.
2. Replace eval of file contents with include of the validated path.

**Verification:** Request `view_help.php` with an id containing ../ and confirm a Not Found response.

**Reference:** [CWE-98](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/98.html)

---

<a id="m-078"></a>
#### M-078 — Re-encode images and store under random names

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-078](#f-078) — Image upload keeps original name without re-encoding

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-016](#w-016)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-434](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/434.html) - Unrestricted Upload of File with Dangerous Type

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/upload/source/high.php:20`

**How:**

1. Re-encode uploaded images to strip any embedded content.
2. Store them under random names outside executable paths.

**Verification:** Upload an image containing appended text and confirm that the stored file no longer contains it.

**Reference:** [CWE-434: Unrestricted Upload of File with Dangerous Type](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/434.html)

---

<a id="m-079"></a>
#### M-079 — Store security level server-side

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-079](#f-079) — Security controls gated by client-controlled cookie

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-565](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/565.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `dvwa/includes/dvwaPage.inc.php:205`

**How:**

1. Store the security level in the server-side session or config, set only by authorized users.
2. Ignore the security cookie for selecting code paths.

**Verification:** Send Cookie `security=low` while session level is impossible and confirm impossible code path runs.

**Reference:** [CWE-565](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/565.html)

---

<a id="m-080"></a>
#### M-080 — Authorize against server-side session user

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-080](#f-080) — Profile authorization trusts client user_id cookie

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-565](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/565.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/bac/source/low.php:33`

**How:**

1. Compare \$id with \$current_user_id derived from `dvwaCurrentUser()`, not with `$_COOKIE['user_id']`.
2. Use a prepared statement for the profile query.

**Verification:** Set the `user_id` cookie to 1 as a non-admin, request `user_id=1`, and confirm access is denied.

**Reference:** [CWE-565](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/565.html)

---

<a id="m-081"></a>
#### M-081 — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-081](#f-081) — IDOR guarded by static public token

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-004](#w-004)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) - Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key (IDOR)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `vulnerabilities/bac/source/medium.php:26`

**How:**

1. Remove the static token check and require \$id == \$current_user_id, or an admin role read from the database.
2. Remove the hint comment from the response.

**Verification:** Request another user's ID with `token=user_token` and confirm access is denied.

**Reference:** [CWE-639: Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html)

---

<a id="m-083"></a>
#### M-083 — Re-check current roles and account status when a session or token is used

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-100](#f-100) — User keeps using a withdrawn right

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-613](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/613.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `vulnerabilities/cryptography/source/token_library_impossible.php:103`

**How:** Re-check current roles and account status when a session or token is used

1. Add a regression test (or CI check) that fails on the vulnerable pattern and passes once the fix is in place.

**Verification:** Re-run the security scan; the CWE-613 finding at `vulnerabilities/cryptography/source/token_library_impossible.php:103` is cleared, and the regression test passes.

## 11. Out of Scope

### Not Covered by This Method

An automated, AI-assisted threat model built from the repository's source code and configuration - static analysis only, no dynamic or penetration testing. It models the system as built rather than as designed. It does not replace a threat-modeling session with the team: business context, design intent, runtime behaviour and production configuration are not covered, and each finding still needs confirmation in the deployed system.

- Design intent and the reasoning behind it - no design documents, ADRs or workshop context are read.
- Business processes and user journeys that leave the code.
- Runtime behaviour, deployment topology and production-only configuration.
- External and organizational controls.

Treat this report as review input, not sign-off.

### Excluded from This Assessment

The following items are **explicitly excluded** from this threat model. Findings against these areas should be tracked separately.

- Third-party hosted dependencies and SaaS endpoints
- Browser runtime vulnerabilities and end-user device security
- Operating system kernel and container runtime
- Underlying network infrastructure (DNS, BGP, ISP)
- Physical security of hosting facilities
- Opt-in threat actors, not assessed: malicious insiders with repository or pipeline access; malicious insiders with production access; attackers holding a user's device. Enable them with `enable:` in `.appsec/actors.yaml`.

## Appendix: Verification Items

Verification checks fixed security properties item by item (catalog version 6). Results per item and component in this run: 12 findings · 1 controls · 4 no finding · 8 undecidable · 77 not applicable. A control means that the examined code enforces the property at every place it applies; a partial, undecidable or no-finding result is never a control, and neither is an item this run did not examine.

Other outcomes by component, plus items this run did not examine. No finding does not prove a control.

- **AuthNRoutes** — Not examined: <a id="tc-authn-01"></a>TC-AUTHN-01 Sensitive routes, operations, and channels require authentication
- **AuthN** — Not applicable for all components: <a id="tc-authn-02"></a>TC-AUTHN-02 Tokens are verified with a pinned algorithm and a server-held key, <a id="tc-authn-03"></a>TC-AUTHN-03 Token expiry, issuer, and audience are validated, <a id="tc-authn-06"></a>TC-AUTHN-06 SSO and OAuth sign-in binds each response to its request and validates the identity it receives; Not applicable: TC-AUTHN-04 (MariaDB Database, CI/CD Pipeline), TC-AUTHN-05 (MariaDB Database, CI/CD Pipeline), TC-AUTHN-07 (MariaDB Database, CI/CD Pipeline)
- **SessionMgmt** — Not examined: <a id="tc-session-01"></a>TC-SESSION-01 Session tokens are protected in storage, transport, lifetime, and logout
- **AuthZ** — Not applicable for all components: <a id="tc-authz-01"></a>TC-AUTHZ-01 Object access checks the owner, <a id="tc-authz-02"></a>TC-AUTHZ-02 Privileged operations check the role or permission, <a id="tc-authz-03"></a>TC-AUTHZ-03 Request bodies cannot set privileged fields, <a id="tc-authz-04"></a>TC-AUTHZ-04 Tenant data stays isolated, <a id="tc-authz-06"></a>TC-AUTHZ-06 Callers cannot read or change data beyond their rights; Not applicable: TC-AUTHZ-05 (MariaDB Database, CI/CD Pipeline)
- **InputVal** — Not applicable for all components: <a id="tc-input-01"></a>TC-INPUT-01 Database queries are parameterized, <a id="tc-input-02"></a>TC-INPUT-02 NoSQL predicates are not built from input, <a id="tc-input-06"></a>TC-INPUT-06 Parsers reject external entities and unsafe deserialization, <a id="tc-input-07"></a>TC-INPUT-07 Input cannot take control of what the server executes, stores, or reaches; Not applicable: TC-INPUT-03 (MariaDB Database, CI/CD Pipeline), TC-INPUT-04 (MariaDB Database, CI/CD Pipeline), TC-INPUT-05 (MariaDB Database, CI/CD Pipeline), TC-FILE-01 (MariaDB Database, CI/CD Pipeline), TC-FILE-02 (MariaDB Database, CI/CD Pipeline)
- **DataProt** — Not applicable for all components: <a id="tc-data-01"></a>TC-DATA-01 Responses contain only intended fields, <a id="tc-data-05"></a>TC-DATA-05 Sensitive data cannot reach anyone not entitled to it; No finding, no control: TC-DATA-04 (CI/CD Pipeline)
- **Crypto** — Not examined: <a id="tc-crypto-01"></a>TC-CRYPTO-01 Cryptographic primitives are current and not hand-rolled, <a id="tc-crypto-02"></a>TC-CRYPTO-02 Secrets and tokens use cryptographic randomness and are not derived from public data
- **FrontendSec** — Not examined: <a id="tc-front-01"></a>TC-FRONT-01 Browser protections are configured: CSP, framing, CORS, subresource integrity, message origins, <a id="tc-mobile-01"></a>TC-MOBILE-01 Mobile WebViews and inter-process interfaces do not expose privileged functions
- **Audit** — Not examined: <a id="tc-audit-01"></a>TC-AUDIT-01 Security-relevant actions are logged, and logs and errors do not leak data
- **Availability** — Not examined: <a id="tc-avail-01"></a>TC-AVAIL-01 Expensive and authentication endpoints are rate-limited and input-driven work is bounded
- **BusinessLogic** — Not examined: <a id="tc-logic-01"></a>TC-LOGIC-01 Business values and workflow order are decided on the server
- **AI** — Not applicable for all components: <a id="tc-ai-01"></a>TC-AI-01 Untrusted content cannot steer the model into privileged actions, <a id="tc-ai-02"></a>TC-AI-02 Model output is validated before it reaches interpreters, browsers, or structured sinks, <a id="tc-ai-03"></a>TC-AI-03 Model-selected tools, objects, and destinations are authorized and restricted, <a id="tc-ai-04"></a>TC-AI-04 Retrieval and memory respect the caller's access, <a id="tc-ai-05"></a>TC-AI-05 The model cannot be made to act or disclose beyond what the caller may
- **Infra** — No finding, no control: <a id="tc-infra-02"></a>TC-INFRA-02 Data and admin services are not publicly exposed; Not examined: <a id="tc-infra-03"></a>TC-INFRA-03 Service identities and cloud permissions follow least privilege
- **SupplyChain** — Not applicable: <a id="tc-supply-02"></a>TC-SUPPLY-02 Dependencies are updated automatically; No finding, no control: <a id="tc-supply-03"></a>TC-SUPPLY-03 Install and build scripts cannot run untrusted code; Not examined: <a id="tc-ci-02"></a>TC-CI-02 Untrusted event data and forks cannot execute in privileged workflows
- **DevTooling** — No finding, no control: <a id="tc-devagent-01"></a>TC-DEVAGENT-01 Coding agents run sandboxed and with approvals; Not applicable: <a id="tc-devagent-02"></a>TC-DEVAGENT-02 Agent tools, MCP servers, hooks, and permission rules are not auto-trusted or over-broad

### Contribution of analysis steps

Source anchors count locations, not independent weaknesses. Different anchors can describe the same problem. Semantic novelty and missing measurements are shown as unknown. Review confirmations do not establish an executed attack.

| Step | Additional locations | Repeated locations | New distinct problems | Stronger evidence | Contradictions | Recorded cost (USD) |
|---------------|--------------------:|------------------:|---------------------:|-----------------:|--------------:|-------------------:|
| Survey | 88 | unknown | unknown | unknown | unknown | 1.04 |
| Verification | 8 | 14 | unknown | unknown | unknown | 2.30 |
| Evidence review | unknown | unknown | 0 | 122 | 3 | 1.66 |
| Abuse cases | 1 | unknown | unknown | 1 | 0 | 0.37 |

Verification is compared with the original survey, including locations preserved inside consolidated findings. Abuse cases are compared with the findings immediately before their checks. Evidence review counts its recorded decisions. Costs include charged failed attempts when the run can attribute them; subscription use or unavailable accounting is unknown.

<a id="appendix-a-vektor-taxonomy"></a>
## Appendix A — Attack Vector Taxonomy

This appendix defines the attacker-starting-position labels used in the Top Threats table and throughout [§8 Findings Register](#8-findings-register). Each label answers the question *what does the attacker need before the exploit begins?*

<a id="vektor-internet-anon"></a>
### Internet Anon

**Attacker position:** Unauthenticated attacker from the public internet · **Breach distance:** 1

**Preconditions:**

- Endpoint is reachable from the internet (no IP allowlist, no VPN)
- No authentication middleware blocks the request

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [CWE-306](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/306.html) · [CWE-327](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/327.html) · [CWE-611](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/611.html) · [CWE-918](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/918.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A01:2025, A05:2025, A07:2025

<a id="vektor-internet-user"></a>
### Internet User

**Attacker position:** Any authenticated low-privilege user (valid JWT / session) · **Breach distance:** 2

**Preconditions:**

- Attacker has signed up or otherwise obtained a valid user session
- Endpoint is behind auth but not behind role/admin checks

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-434](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/434.html) · [CWE-611](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/611.html) · [CWE-918](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/918.html) · [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) · [CWE-287](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/287.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A01:2025, A06:2025, A02:2025

<a id="vektor-internet-priv-user"></a>
### Internet Priv User

**Attacker position:** Authenticated admin-level user (JWT with admin role or equivalent) · **Breach distance:** 2

**Preconditions:**

- Attacker holds admin credentials or has elevated privileges
- Endpoint gated on admin role but still exploitable once reached

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [CWE-94](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/94.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A01:2025

<a id="vektor-victim-required"></a>
### Victim-Required

**Attacker position:** Attacker needs victim interaction - social engineering, crafted link, or live session · **Breach distance:** 2

**Preconditions:**

- Victim must click a link, load a page, or have an active session
- Applies to XSS, CSRF, click-jacking, open redirect

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) · [CWE-601](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/601.html) · [CWE-1021](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1021.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A01:2025, A05:2025

<a id="vektor-build-time"></a>
### Build-Time

**Attacker position:** Attacker controls a build input - CI runner, dependency, base image, or external data fetched during build · **Breach distance:** 3

**Preconditions:**

- Compromise of a dependency, registry, or base image
- OR compromise of a CI runner with write access to artifacts

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-506](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/506.html) · [CWE-829](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/829.html) · [CWE-1039](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1039.html) · [CWE-1104](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1104.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A08:2025

<a id="vektor-repo-read"></a>
### Repo-Read

**Attacker position:** Attacker gains read access to source repository (leaked clone, forked fork, insider, compromised developer workstation) · **Breach distance:** 3

**Preconditions:**

- Read access to the source tree at or after commit time
- No runtime exploit needed - the vulnerability is the content of the repo

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) · [CWE-312](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/312.html) · [CWE-540](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/540.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A04:2025, A07:2025

<a id="vektor-n-a"></a>
### Architectural (no runtime entry point)

**Attacker position:** Architectural / meta-finding - no runtime entry point, the finding describes a design defect aggregating multiple code-level findings

**Preconditions:**

- Finding is AF-NNN (architectural) rather than F-NNN (code-level)
